# A torsion-free counterexample to reduced Baum–Connes injectivity

Coordinate `EN-116-000-0000-2-2026-PR-T4-UK-EX-M3-C-TO-H-0590` | `PM-285.1` | status ACTIVE | original slug `A-Torsion-Free-Counterexample-to-Reduced-Baum-Connes-Injectivity-September-23-2026` | date 2026-09-23 | derived from the openai/math preprint A-Torsion-Free-Counterexample-to-Reduced-Baum-Connes-Injectivity-September-23-2026, modified (Apache-2.0). Publication status: DRAFT.

```json
{
  "abstract_verbatim": "We disprove rational injectivity of the coefficient-free reduced Baum–Connes assembly map for torsion-free groups. Specifically, we construct a finitely generated torsion-free discrete group whose degree-zero assembly map has a kernel class of infinite order.",
```

<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We disprove rational injectivity of the coefficient-free reduced Baum–Connes assembly map for torsion-free groups. Specifically, we construct a finitely generated torsion-free discrete group whose degree-zero assembly map has a kernel class of infinite order.

- **claim-reduced-assembly-kernel** (counterexample, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): There exist a finitely generated torsion-free discrete group $G_{\mathrm{inj}}$, an injective homomorphism $i:\mathbb Z^2\to G_{\mathrm{inj}}$, and a class $c\in H^2(BG_{\mathrm{inj}};\mathbb Z)$ such that $$\left\langle(Bi)^*c,[T^2]\right\rangle=1,\qquad i_*(\beta)=0\quad\text{in }K_0(C_r^*G_{\mathrm{inj}}),$$ where $\beta$ is a rank-zero Bott generator of $K_0(C_r^*\mathbb Z^2)$. In particular, $h=(Bi)_*[D_{T^2}]$ has infinite order in $K_0(BG_{\mathrm{inj}})$, while its reduced analytic assembly image is zero. Here $[D_{T^2}]$ is the spin Dirac $K$-homology class of the oriented torus.
  - Explanation: Constructs an infinite-order geometric class with zero reduced assembly image, detected by an integral torus pairing.
- **claim-coefficient-pools** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-coefficient-pools)): For sufficiently large $s$, the injections can be chosen so that, within every fixed family and extra slice and on either side, the following holds. If $n$ is its number of slots, $\zeta$ is any nonzero output linear functional on an ordinary $s$-block, and $b\le t\le s$, then each restricted input functional $$(\zeta K_\nu)\|_{\mathbb F_2^t}$$ occurs on at most $2^{1-b}n$ slots. All distinct names have distinct coefficients in every field in which they occur. The analogous bound holds on the initial A field for every prefix length between $b$ and $r$.
  - Explanation: Chooses distinct field coefficients while controlling collisions of restricted input functionals.
- **claim-incidence-inversion** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-incidence-inversion)): For every choice of tables the auxiliary prescription has exactly one edge at every specified endpoint and slot. In a fixed extra slice it is biregular of degrees $d,D$. A uniform endpoint with a fixed slot, after fixing its extra value when necessary, is parametrized bijectively by the free label head and all free raw $u_A,u_B$ coordinates at the common cutoff. Consequently those raw coordinates are independent uniform bits.
  - Explanation: Triangular inversion gives unique auxiliary edges and identifies independent uniform raw coordinates.
- **claim-gate-deletions** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-gate-deletions)): For every fixed set of tables, the fraction deleted for gate reasons from either state space at any height is at most $$\label{eq:gate-budget} \varepsilon_{\rm gate}=600T2^bD\,2^{-8s}.$$ On a surviving edge both auxiliary forced choices were unnecessary, so the edge satisfies the universal padded equations <a href="#eq:universal-edge-equations" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:universal-edge-equations">[eq:universal-edge-equations]</a>. At each surviving slot, separately among its full A source blocks and its full B source blocks, fewer than $.005$ of the updates are unavailable because of a closed gate or a marked or following deterministic identity rule. The last three source gates and the actual prefix gate are open.
  - Explanation: Bounds gate deletions uniformly and ensures surviving edges obey the universal padded equations.
- **claim-padded-prefix-recovery** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-tower-recovery)): Let two immersed paths in the gate-surviving graph have the same oriented label word. At corresponding A positions of radius at least $2j+1$, both raw streams agree through $r+js$ bits. At corresponding B positions of radius at least $2j$, both raw streams agree through $js$ bits. These statements compare padded coordinates, so the positions may have arbitrarily different heights.
  - Explanation: Equal immersed words recover equal padded prefixes at corresponding interior positions across arbitrary heights.
- **claim-separation-deletions** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-separation-deletions)): The fraction deleted for separation from either side at any height is at most $$\label{eq:separation-budget} \varepsilon_{\rm sep} =2\left( \frac{2^{-Mb/2}}{1-2^{-Mb/2}} +\frac{2^{-\eta_{\rm sep} H_0}}{1-2^{-\eta_{\rm sep} b}} \right).$$
  - Explanation: Bounds deletions that exclude ambiguity between a shorter state and a longer zero extension.
- **claim-deterministic-tower** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-tower-labels)): For every fixed finite type set and every sufficiently large $s$ as above, the construction has the following properties.* 1. *The auxiliary state sizes are <a href="#eq:state-sizes" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:state-sizes">[eq:state-sizes]</a>. In a fixed extra slice an incidence has degrees $d,D$. Without slicing, its smaller side acquires the extra factor $2^b$ and its larger side does not. The total degrees are at most $T2^b d$ on A and $T2^bD$ on B.* 2. *The labeling is folded: no two outgoing oriented edges at a vertex have the same label. Its signed alphabet satisfies $$\label{eq:sharp-alphabet} \|\mathcal S\|\le 2TM2^b d2^r, \qquad \frac{\sqrt{\|\mathcal S\|}}q \le 2^{-.000025s+O(\log s)+O_{T,b}(1)}\longrightarrow0.$$* 3. *At every height the fraction of removed vertices on each side is at most $\varepsilon_{\rm gate}+\varepsilon_{\rm sep}$. This bound can be made arbitrarily small, uniformly over heights and all table choices. The separate-parity and terminal gate conclusions of Lemma <a href="#lem:gate-deletions" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:gate-deletions">4</a> hold.* 4. *Every immersed path in $X^{\rm good}$ starting at height $i$ and having length at least $\lceil1.002m_i\rceil$ is the unique immersed path with that oriented word anywhere in $X^{\rm good}$. This is a global assertion over all heights and components.
  - Explanation: Combines degree and alphabet bounds, small deletion budgets, foldedness, and global uniqueness of long immersed words.
- **claim-fresh-matchings** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-fresh-matching-coordinates)): Fix a family occurrence, an extra slice, and every table before its last common source block. Under the independent uniform current-table law, its auxiliary incidence is randomized by independent perfect matchings between pairs of clusters of $2^t$ slot incidences, one matching for each full final-prefix table key. Each possible edge in a cluster pair is permitted. No later table affects this incidence.
  - Explanation: Expresses the current auxiliary incidence through independent complete matchings on slot clusters.
- **claim-conditional-fourier** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-conditional-fourier)): Conditional on data $C$, let $U\in\mathbb F_2^t$ and $V\in\mathbb F_2^w$ be independent uniform vectors, independent also of auxiliary data $W$. For $a$ in a set of $q_0$ slots let $K_a:\mathbb F_2^t\to\mathbb F_2^w$ be linear, let $o_a=o_a(C,W)$, and let $f_a(C,z)$ have no separate dependence on $U,V,W$. Suppose every class of slots with a common value of $\zeta K_a$, for any nonzero functional $\zeta\in(\mathbb F_2^w)^*$, has at most $\theta q_0$ members. Set $$F=\frac1{q_0}\sum_a f_a(C,V+K_aU+o_a),\qquad \mu(C)=\frac1{q_0}\sum_a2^{-w}\sum_z f_a(C,z).$$ Then $\mathbb E(F\mid C,W,U)=\mu(C)$ and $$\label{eq:fourier-conditional-bound} \mathbb E\|F-\mu(C)\|^2 \leq\theta\,\mathbb E\left[\frac1{q_0}\sum_a2^{-w} \sum_z\|f_a(C,z)\|^2\right].$$
  - Explanation: Character orthogonality converts bounded functional multiplicity into a conditional variance bound.
- **claim-conditional-mean** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-conditional-mean)): For every fixed earlier-table history, every marked cutoff, and every extra-bit slice, $$\\|\mathsf{M}:1_B^\perp\longrightarrow1_A^\perp\\| \leq\eta:=\sqrt{5\theta}<3/5.$$ The same bound holds for the complete adjacent-height incidence.
  - Explanation: Orthogonal prefix bands and the current terminal average give a uniform centered mean norm bound.
- **claim-sequential-selection** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-sequential-law)): There is a probability law on all the permutation tables such that every auxiliary incidence has centered singular norm at most the fixed number $\rho<1$ in <a href="#eq:uniform-incidence-gap" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:uniform-incidence-gap">[eq:uniform-incidence-gap]</a>. At each individual source-block update, conditional on all earlier updates, any joint test of $k$ specified current tables has its probability increased by at most $e^{\varepsilon_s k}$ relative to independent uniform current tables. The bound is uniform in the earlier history and the height, with $\varepsilon_s\to0$.
  - Explanation: Selects expanding incidences throughout the tower while retaining a uniform bound for joint current-table tests.
- **claim-coherent-junction** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-coherent-junction)): At the identified endpoints of a minimal coherent return, the two outward incident coefficient names are different.
  - Explanation: Minimality and foldedness force different coefficient names at the coherent junction.
- **claim-unique-reconstruction** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-pruning-reconstruction)): Fix a coherent-path pattern with different junction coefficients, and fix its free endpoint u-bits. For any realization of the universal tables there is at most one assignment of vertex states realizing that pattern as a coherent path. If its endpoint scales are $H_-$ and $H_+$, the number of free bits is at most $$\label{eq:pruning-free-bits} (H_-+H_+)/2-h.$$ For a theta pattern, there is at most one assignment of states and there are no free bits. During either reconstruction all inputs and keys queried at a source block are determined before that block’s tables are exposed.
  - Explanation: Reconstructs each fixed candidate uniquely with predictable queries and bounds its remaining free endpoint bits.
- **claim-predictable-rank** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-pruning-rank-process)): Suppose that, at each of finitely many source blocks, a test is selected from $\mathcal F_{<t}$, and its product-law probability is at most $2^{-R_t}$. The nonnegative integers $R_t$ and $k_t$ are $\mathcal F_{<t}$-measurable. If an event $A$ implies that all tests pass, that $\sum_tR_t\geq R$, and that $\sum_tk_t\leq K$, then $$\mathbb P(A)\leq \exp(\epsilon_sK)2^{-R}.$$ The event $A$ may additionally require later minimality, missed-gate counts, and full consistency of the reconstructed vertex states.
  - Explanation: A nonnegative process bounds the probability of passing predictable tests with accumulated rank and table budgets.
- **claim-terminal-compatibility** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-pruning-terminal-codimension)): If both incident common cutoffs equal the receiving vertex’s own cutoff, the terminal constraints from two independent incident evaluations have product-law probability exactly $2^{-\lambda(v)}$. If each incident common cutoff is at most $b$ bits below that receiver cutoff, one can test an event on those two current tables with probability at most $$2^{-\max\{0,\lambda(v)-4b\}}.$$ The keys must be distinct between the tested edge occurrences; the input arguments must be known before the current tables are exposed, and the required terminal gates must be open. Only the actual uniform active prefix of a partial permutation is used in this statement.
  - Explanation: Computes terminal compatibility probabilities from active dimensions, with an allowance for adjacent cutoffs.
- **claim-repeated-key-prefixes** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-pruning-key-prefix)): Suppose two distinct tested terminal edge occurrences at the same source block use the same table key. Their receiving vertices have the same side and agree in raw u and v through available aligned depth at least $$H_*-8s,$$ where $H_*$ is the minimum scale among the vertices of the counted path or theta. At any nonterminal update, equality of keys likewise identifies both receiver prefixes through the preceding complete receiver block.
  - Explanation: Equal complete table keys identify receiver prefixes before the current output is exposed.
- **claim-end-guard** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-pruning-end-guard)): In every minimal coherent return, all terminal keys tested outside the end guard are distinct at each source block.
  - Explanation: A repeated terminal key outside the guards would create a shorter coherent return.
- **claim-coherent-return-count** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-pruning-return-count)): For sufficiently large $s/b$ and then $H_0/s$, the expected number of minimal coherent returns based on either side at height $i$, counted over all bands containing them, is at most $$2^{.975H_i}.$$ This counts oriented rooted paths, including repetitions of vertices and edges.
  - Explanation: Combines pattern enumeration, free coordinates, and terminal rank to bound expected rooted coherent returns.
- **claim-base-girth** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-pruning-base-girth)): No nonempty cyclically reduced closed path based at height $i$ in $X^{(1)}$ has length at most $.93m_i$.
  - Explanation: A short closed path would contain a coherent return removed by the initial pruning.
- **claim-theta-key-separation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-pruning-theta-keys)): Let a theta in $X^{(1)}$ have $e<1.52m_i$ edges and a root at height $i$, and set $H=H_i$. At every source-block update, the terminal keys used at its distinct vertices are distinct. If its two branch vertices have the same side, any key shared by their branch triples at a depth beyond $.65H$ is impossible, apart from a fixed initial/final block allowance that is omitted below.
  - Explanation: Survival after coherent-return pruning forbids terminal key collisions and late shared branch keys.
- **claim-branch-triple-ranks** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-pruning-triple-ranks)): One branch-vertex relation in an ordinary full block with three open gates has product-law probability $2^{-s}$. If two same-side branch vertices have different unordered key triples, their two relations have joint probability $2^{-2s}$. If their triples are the same, it is always safe to charge just one relation.
  - Explanation: Distinct current keys yield full-field branch constraints; different triples permit separate exclusive-table tests.
- **claim-bicycle-count** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-pruning-theta-count)): For sufficiently large allowed parameters, the expected number of rooted embedded bicycles in $X^{(1)}$ of length below $1.52m_i$, based on either side at height $i$, is at most $$2^{.89H_i}.$$
  - Explanation: Terminal and branch ranks bound the expected number of short embedded bicycles remaining after coherent-return pruning.
- **claim-heisenberg-law-bicycle** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-heisenberg-law)): Let $W$ be a nonempty closed edge path in $X$, reduced as a based edge path: no two consecutive traversals in its linear ordering are mutually inverse. Suppose the word of $W$ is a $\mathsf H$-law. Then its support contains a bicycle $B$ such that every edge of $B$ is traversed at least four times by $W$. Consequently, $$\ell(W)\geq 4\ell(B).$$ In particular, if every bicycle in the support of $W$ has length at least $T$, then $\ell(W)\geq4T$.
  - Explanation: Edge balance and paired-edge intervals isolate a bicycle whose edges all have heavy traversal multiplicity.
- **claim-pruned-old-graph** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-pruning)): Given any $\varepsilon>0$, the allowed parameters and the universal tables can be chosen so that the additional two deletions in this Section remove less than an $\varepsilon$ fraction of each $\mathscr A_i$ and $\mathscr B_i$. In the surviving induced old graph $X_{\mathrm{old}}$:* 1. *the folded labeling, padded-prefix recovery of Lemma <a href="#lem:tower-recovery" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:tower-recovery">5</a>, and global word uniqueness of Proposition <a href="#prop:tower-labels" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:tower-labels">7</a> remain valid;* 2. *a nonempty cyclically reduced closed path based at height $i$ has length greater than $.93m_i$;* 3. *every bicycle containing a vertex of height $i$ has length at least $1.52m_i$;* 4. *every nonempty cyclically reduced closed path based at height $i$ with length at most $6.04m_i$ is not a law in the independent edge variables of $\mathsf H$.* *The expansion conclusions for the undeleted incidences hold simultaneously, and the degrees remain fixed along the tower.
  - Explanation: Chooses a simultaneous small-loss realization with girth, bicycle exclusion, nonlaw bounds, and undeleted expansion.
- **claim-supported-spreading** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-supported-spreading)): For sufficiently large initial height, the family of projections $$\lambda(y)^jE_i\lambda(y)^{-j},\qquad \left\lvert j\right\rvert\leq J_i,$$ is pairwise orthogonal. Consequently $$E_iT_i^*T_iE_i=E_i,\qquad P_i=T_iE_iT_i^*$$ is a projection, and $$\label{eq:spread-trace} \tau_{\mathsf H}(P_i)=\tau_{\mathsf H}(E_i) \leq C_R2^{-700H_i}.$$ If $f$ is supported in both enlarged rectangles at heights $i$ and $k$, and $g$ in both enlarged rectangles at heights $k$ and $j$, then $$(T_i fT_k^*)(T_k gT_j^*)=T_i(fg)T_j^*,\qquad (T_i fT_k^*)^*=T_k f^*T_i^*,\qquad \left\lVert T_i fT_k^*\right\rVert\leq\left\lVert f\right\rVert.$$
  - Explanation: Separated torus translates make spreading isometric on the carrier and preserve supported products, adjoints, and norm bounds.
- **claim-simultaneously-sampled-symbols** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-sampled-symbols)): Fix b, a finite matrix dimension, a positive accuracy $\eta$, and a finite list of compact smooth symbol profiles and height selections as above. The list may include cutoffs, both parities, the initial-height selection, and any finite mesh of the adjacent rescaling and rotation families. Allocate a prescribed incidence type for every required same or adjacent layer symbol. For sufficiently large s and then sufficiently large initial height, the surviving base graph admits a voltage on every edge and finite-net matrix weights such that the following hold simultaneously.* 1. *The voltage cover has no nontrivial cyclically reduced closed walk of length at most $6.03m_i$ based at an old vertex of height i.* 2. *All weighted blocks may use the common denominator q. If $\mathcal V_{\tau,ik}$ is that normalized weighted convolution block and the prescribed symbol is $S_{\tau,ik}=T_if_{\tau,ik}T_k^*$, then $$\left\lVert\mathcal V_{\tau,ik} -U_{\tau,ik}\otimes S_{\tau,ik}\right\rVert\leq\eta$$ in the full regular operator norm, at every height. The tensor notation here means that each scalar incidence entry multiplies the same matrix-valued deck convolution symbol.* 3. *Weights are read from one finite set, independent of height. Appending their net indices to the existing edge labels preserves the labelled-graph uniqueness properties and gives $$\log_2\left\lvert\mathcal A\right\rvert \leq1.00005s+O(\log s)+O_{b,\eta,\mathrm{profiles}}(1) <\log_2(q^2)$$ for sufficiently large s. In particular $\sqrt{\left\lvert\mathcal A\right\rvert}/q$ tends to zero as s increases after the fixed data. Zero-weight edges are retained in the voltage cover.
  - Explanation: Selects voltages and finite-net weights giving full regular norm approximation and exclusion of short closed lifts.
- **claim-fourier-envelope** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-fourier-envelope)): Let $\left\lvert i-k\right\rvert\leq1$, and let $f$ belong to the profile family <a href="#eq:profile-family" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:profile-family">[eq:profile-family]</a>, expressed at reference height $i$. Write $$T_i fT_k^*=\sum_{n,l,c}A_{n,l,c}\lambda(x^ny^lz^c), \qquad N=N_i,\quad Z=Z_i,\quad J=J_i.$$ For every pair of nonnegative integers $A,P$, with $P\geq3$, $$\label{eq:weighted-central-envelope} \sum_c(1+\left\lvert c\right\rvert/Z)^A\left\lVert A_{n,l,c}\right\rVert \leq C_{A,P}N^{-1}(1+\left\lvert n\right\rvert/N)^{-P} (1+J\left\lVert n\alpha\right\rVert_{\mathbb R/\mathbb Z})^{-3}, \qquad \left\lvert l\right\rvert\leq C_bJ,$$ and the coefficients vanish for larger $\left\lvert l\right\rvert$. The constants are uniform in height and in the compact profile family. In particular these symbols have a uniformly bounded Fourier one-norm. For each $\eta>0$ they can be truncated, with one-norm error at most $\eta$, to $$\label{eq:fourier-box} \left\lvert n\right\rvert\leq L_\eta N,\qquad \left\lvert l\right\rvert\leq C_bJ,\qquad \left\lvert c\right\rvert\leq L_\eta Z,$$ where $L_\eta$ is independent of height.
  - Explanation: Separates horizontal frequency decay from central tails and yields a uniform Fourier mass bound and finite truncations.
- **claim-finite-product-sampler** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-product-sampler)): For every fixed $\eta>0$, each symbol in Lemma <a href="#lem:fourier-envelope" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:fourier-envelope">30</a> has a finite distribution of pairs $(W,v)$, with $W\in M_\ell(\mathbb C)$ and $v=x^ny^lz^c\in\mathsf H$, satisfying $$\left\lVert W\right\rVert\leq M_\eta,\qquad \left\lVert\mathbb E[W\lambda(v)]-T_ifT_k^*\right\rVert_1\leq\eta.$$ The horizontal coordinates n and l are independent. Their maximal atoms satisfy $$\label{eq:horizontal-atoms} \sup_n\Pr(n)=O_b(J_i/N_i),\qquad \sup_l\Pr(l)=O_b(J_i^{-1}).$$ All possible group coordinates, including those of inverses, are bounded by $2^{a_0H_i}$ for a fixed exponent $a_0$, for example $a_0=502$, after increasing the initial height. The weights can additionally be restricted to one finite adjoint-closed net, at the expense of any prescribed further one-norm error. All constants and the net are independent of height.
  - Explanation: Independent diffuse horizontal marginals dominate Fourier mass; conditional central sampling carries the bounded matrix weight.
- **claim-polynomial-avoidance** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-polynomial-avoidance)): Use independent edge draws as in Lemma <a href="#lem:product-sampler" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:product-sampler">31</a>. For a fixed base walk, reuse its one voltage variable on every occurrence of an unoriented edge and use its inverse in the reverse direction. If the associated word is not a law in $\mathsf H$, the probability that its holonomy is the identity is at most $$C_b2^{-50H_{\min}},$$ where $H_{\min}$ is the smallest reference height of an edge in the walk. The estimate is unaffected by matrix weights, including zero weights.
  - Explanation: A nonzero horizontal or central polynomial bounds identity holonomy using independent horizontal maximal atoms.
- **claim-heisenberg-box-norm** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-heisenberg-folner-test)): Suppose a finite set of group elements and their inverses have coordinates bounded by $2^{a_0H}$, with $H\geq1$. For every fixed B there is a finite box $\mathcal B_H\subset\mathsf H$, of cardinality at most $2^{K_0H}$, such that $$\frac{\left\lvert\mathcal B_H\triangle v\mathcal B_H\right\rvert} {\left\lvert\mathcal B_H\right\rvert}\leq C2^{-BH}$$ for all these elements. The exponent $K_0$ depends on $a_0,B$, but not on the number of elements.* *Let $A$ be a selfadjoint finite matrix of regular convolution sums whose convolution support is contained in the finite set just specified, with any fixed matrix amplification. Let R bound its absolute coefficient row sums; by selfadjointness it also bounds the column sums. If $Q_H$ retains the deck coordinates in $\mathcal B_H$, then $$\label{eq:full-regular-norm-test} \left\lVert Q_HAQ_H\right\rVert\leq\left\lVert A\right\rVert \leq\left\lVert Q_HAQ_H\right\rVert+C2^{-BH}R.$$ The same statement holds for rectangular matrices after selfadjoint off-diagonal doubling, using the larger row or column coefficient bound.
  - Explanation: Finite Heisenberg boxes control translation boundary loss and approximate the full matrix convolution norm.
- **claim-support-cycle-rank** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-moment-support-rank)): Let S be a finite connected metric graph of total length at most Kg and girth greater than g. Then $$\dim H_1(S;\mathbb Q)\leq \max\{1,\lceil20K\rceil\}^2.$$
  - Explanation: A separated metric net maps a bounded finite graph onto the support homology, bounding its cycle rank.
- **claim-repeated-edge-walk-count** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-repeated-edge-count)): Let a graph have maximum degree $\Delta\geq1$. Consider length-$2h$ closed walks from a fixed vertex, with no unoriented edge occurring exactly once. Multiple edges are treated as distinct edges. If every support under consideration has cycle rank at most R, their number is at most $$[72(2R+2)^2\Delta]^h.$$
  - Explanation: Encodes the rooted core and attached tree excursions to count closed walks without singleton edges.
- **claim-centered-block-concentration** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-centered-voltage-block)): Fix a surviving incidence type and one of its same or adjacent layer blocks. Assign independent samples from Lemma <a href="#lem:product-sampler" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:product-sampler">31</a> to its unoriented edges. Let $X_{ik}$ be its unnormalized centered convolution matrix. For every $\eta>0$, the choices can be arranged in the order described below so that $$\label{eq:centered-block-tail} \Pr\{\left\lVert X_{ik}\right\rVert>\eta q_{\tau,ik}\}\leq2^{-6H_i},$$ uniformly in height. All estimates are under the independent sampling law, without conditioning on voltage girth.
  - Explanation: Finite regular norm tests and centered trace moments give a height-uniform probability bound without conditioning on voltage girth.
- **claim-private-completion** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-private-completion)): There is a connected, countable, folded labeled graph $X$ containing $X_{\mathrm o}$ as its old subgraph, and an extension of the voltages, with the following properties.* 1. *Each added edge has its own new positive letter, used on no other base edge. Every new vertex has degree two and has only private incident edges. The maximum degree is at most $K_{b,\epsilon}D+6$.* 2. *The function $m$ extends to the new vertices, remains at least $m_{\min}$, and satisfies $\|m(v)-m(w)\|\le a$ on every edge.* 3. *The voltage cover $\widetilde X$, with edges $$\label{eq:voltage-convention} (v,h)\longrightarrow(w,hh_e)$$ over $e\colon v\to w$, is connected. Its deck action is the left action $f(v,h)=(v,fh)$ of $\mathsf H$.* 4. *Every simple cycle $C$ of $\widetilde X$ satisfies $\|C\|>6.03m(v)$ at every vertex $v$ of $C$.* 5. *If two paths in $\widetilde X$ have the same word and their projected occurrences in $X$ differ, neither contains a private edge. If they are immersed and start at $v,w$, their common length is less than both $Lm(v)$ and $Lm(w)$.* *The scale on the cover is the pullback of the scale on $X$.
  - Explanation: Private connecting paths and voltage loops give a connected cover while preserving local scale, girth, and word recovery.
- **claim-word-diagram** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-word-diagram)): If a finite word $w$ is trivial in <a href="#eq:graphical-presentation" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:graphical-presentation">[eq:graphical-presentation]</a>, there is a finite plane multigraph diagram with exterior contour reading the literal word $w$. More precisely, if in the free group on $S$ $$w=\prod_{j=1}^n u_jr_ju_j^{-1},$$ where each $r_j$ is read on a chosen closed source walk, a diagram may be chosen with filling cost at most $\sum_j\|r_j\|$. Conversely, the exterior word of any such diagram is a product of conjugates of its bounded-face words in the free group, using each bounded facial word once with the compatible orientation. Its labeled one-skeleton therefore has a well-defined vertex map to the formal Cayley graph after prescribing the image of one vertex.
  - Explanation: Produces finite plane multigraph fillings with a perimeter budget and identifies exterior words as products of conjugate face words.
- **claim-null-path-closure** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-null-path-closes)): A path in $\widetilde X$ whose label is trivial in $\Gamma$ has equal initial and terminal vertices.
  - Explanation: Minimal filling reductions and the face-arc curvature contradiction force every null-labeled source path to close.
- **claim-graphical-realization** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-graphical-realization)): There is an injective map of vertices and formal oriented edges $$j\colon\widetilde X\longrightarrow\operatorname{Cay}(\Gamma,S)$$ preserving labels and sending $(v_*,1)$ to the identity. The subgroup of vertices over $v_*$ defines an injective homomorphism $\phi\colon\mathsf H\hookrightarrow\Gamma$. If $t_v=j(v,1)$, then $$\label{eq:deck-transport} j(v,h)=\phi(h)t_v.$$ The full group of label-preserving automorphisms of $\widetilde X$ is its deck group. Consequently the distinct translated images, called *sheets*, are indexed by $\Gamma/\phi(\mathsf H)$, and the stabilizer of $g j(\widetilde X)$ under left translation is $g\phi(\mathsf H)g^{-1}$.
  - Explanation: Embeds the voltage cover and its Heisenberg deck group, and identifies translated sheets through their stabilizers.
- **claim-fixed-contact-polygons** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-sheet-polygon)): Let $Q_1,\ldots,Q_k$ be distinct sheets, $k\ge2$, and let $g_i\in Q_i\cap Q_{i+1}$, with indices modulo $k$. Contacts may coincide. Choose a source path in $Q_i$ from $g_{i-1}$ to $g_i$ for each $i$. There is a finite diagram and a choice of these paths, *with every prescribed endpoint $g_i$ fixed*, such that:* 1. *Each side is linearly immersed. After retaining all junction vertices and suppressing other degree-two vertices, every arc has two different projected occurrences in $X$. Each occurrence is immersed, contains no private edge, and has length less than $Lm_Q(v)$ at its initial point. Each entire nonconstant side is the concatenation of its exterior arc occurrences.* 2. *Every bounded face has at least seven arc incidences.* 3. *If $B$ counts exterior arc incidences, counting an exterior bridge twice, then $$\label{eq:polygon-exterior} B\le4k-6.$$* *A diagram with no edges has $B=0$ and all its sides constant. In particular, a nonconstant side cannot have a private vertex as an endpoint. A side whose intrinsic endpoint distance is greater than $3.014m_Q(v)$ at its initial endpoint has at least four arc incidences.
  - Explanation: Reduces sheet polygons while fixing contacts and bounds their exterior comparison arcs by a planar count.
- **claim-allowed-removal-forests** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-allowed-domain-sets)): For an old vertex $v\in Q$, set $$B_Q(v)=\{w\in V(Q):d_Q(v,w)\leq\lfloor\kappa m_Q(v)\rfloor\}.$$ The graphs $\operatorname{Star}_Q(B_Q(v))$ and $\operatorname{Star}_Q(\{w\})$, for a private vertex $w$, are forests. The empty set also has this property.* *After increasing $s$ as permitted by the parameter order, if $B_Q(v)$ contains an old vertex of layer $i$, then $$\left\lvert B_Q(v)\right\rvert\leq 2^{10H_i}. \label{eq:domain-ball-cardinality}$$ A private singleton contains no old vertex.
  - Explanation: Local girth makes allowed incidence neighborhoods forests and bounds the size of an old-centered ball meeting a layer.
- **claim-finite-sheet-peeling** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-sheet-peeling)): Every nonempty finite family $\mathcal F$ has a sheet $Q$ for which $I_{\mathcal F}(Q)$ is either empty, contained in $B_Q(v)$ for some old contact $v$, or equal to a singleton private contact. Consequently the family can be ordered $Q_1,\ldots,Q_n$ with allowed removed sets $R_j$ of those three forms such that $$V(Q_j)\cap\bigcup_{\ell>j}V(Q_\ell)\subseteq R_j. \label{eq:finite-peeling-overlap}$$
  - Explanation: A forbidden polygon argument finds a sheet with overlap inside an allowed removed set, enabling finite peeling.
- **claim-direct-cycle-coordinates** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-sheet-cycle-coordinates)): For every commutative coefficient ring $R$, the sum of the inclusions is an isomorphism $$\Phi_R:\bigoplus_{Q\in\mathcal Q}Z_1(Q;R) \longrightarrow Z_1(\mathcal C;R),\qquad (c_Q)_Q\longmapsto\sum_Qc_Q. \label{eq:sheet-cycle-isomorphism}$$ Both the direct sum and every chain appearing here have finite support.
  - Explanation: Finite relator decompositions give surjectivity; peeling and forest support give uniqueness of sheet cycle coordinates.
- **claim-simultaneous-domains** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-domains)): There are sets $R_Q\subseteq V(Q)$, one for each sheet, such that each $R_Q$ is empty, an old-centered ball $B_Q(v)$, or a private singleton, and the sets $D_Q=V(Q)\setminus R_Q$ have the following properties.* 1. *The physical vertex sets $D_Q$ are pairwise disjoint.* 2. *Put $$E_{\mathrm{int}}= \bigcup_{Q\in\mathcal Q} \{e\in E(Q):\text{both endpoints of }e\text{ lie in }D_Q\}.$$ Every edge in this union has a unique such sheet, and $(V(\mathcal C),E(\mathcal C)\setminus E_{\mathrm{int}})$ is a forest.* 3. *$\operatorname{Star}_Q(R_Q)$ is a forest. If $R_Q$ contains an old vertex of layer $i$, then $\left\lvert R_Q\right\rvert\leq2^{10H_i}$. A private singleton removes no old coordinate.* *No equivariance of the choices $R_Q$ is asserted or needed.
  - Explanation: Finite-coordinate compactness selects disjoint sheet domains whose excluded internal-edge complement is a forest.
- **claim-sheet-incidence-coordinates** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-sheet-incidences)): The map sending a sheet incidence to its physical vertex and its base type is a bijection $$\{(Q,u):Q\in\mathcal Q,\ u\in V(Q)\} \longrightarrow \Gamma\times V(X). \label{eq:sheet-incidence-bijection}$$ In particular, a physical vertex occurs in at most $n_i^{\mathrm{full}}=\left\lvert\mathscr A_i\right\rvert+\left\lvert\mathscr B_i\right\rvert$ old layer-$i$ incidences, and $$n_i^{\mathrm{full}}=(1+2^{2r-s})2^{H_i}\leq C_s2^{H_i}. \label{eq:old-layer-incidences}$$
  - Explanation: The physical vertex and base type determine a unique sheet incidence and bound old-layer multiplicity.
- **claim-exact-cone-chains** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-cone-exact)): For every commutative coefficient ring $R$, the augmented cellular complex $$0\longrightarrow C_2(\mathcal Z;R) \xrightarrow{\partial_2} C_1(\mathcal Z;R) \xrightarrow{\partial_1} C_0(\mathcal Z;R) \xrightarrow{\varepsilon}R\longrightarrow0 \label{eq:exact-cone-complex}$$ is exact. This assertion depends on finite cycle coordinates, not on the compactness step in Proposition <a href="#prop:domains" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:domains">45</a>.
  - Explanation: Finite cycle coordinates prove exactness of the augmented cone chains over any commutative coefficient ring.
- **claim-free-resolution-comparison** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-free-resolution-comparison)): Let $P_\bullet\to M$ and $Q_\bullet\to N$ be exact augmented complexes of free modules over a ring $A$. Every homomorphism $M\to N$ lifts to a chain map $P_\bullet\to Q_\bullet$. Any two such lifts are chain homotopic. In particular, two free resolutions of one module are chain homotopy equivalent, and remain so after tensoring with a right $A$-module or applying $\operatorname{Hom}_A(-,L)$.
  - Explanation: Free-basis lifting and exactness construct comparison maps and homotopies, preserved by tensor and Hom.
- **claim-torsion-freeness** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-no-prime-torsion)): The group $\Gamma$ is torsion-free.
  - Explanation: A prime-order subgroup would have incompatible homology from finite cone chains and its periodic resolution.
- **claim-primitive-heisenberg-detector** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-explicit-heisenberg-detector)): In the coordinates of Equation <a href="#eq:heisenberg-coordinates" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:heisenberg-coordinates">[eq:heisenberg-coordinates]</a>, the integer function $$\omega\bigl((\alpha,\beta,\gamma), (\alpha',\beta',\gamma')\bigr) =\alpha\gamma'+\binom{\alpha}{2}\beta' \label{eq:explicit-heisenberg-cocycle}$$ is a normalized group $2$-cocycle. Its class $c_{\mathsf H}$ evaluates to $-1$ on the torus with ordered generators $(z,x)$.
  - Explanation: An explicit integral cocycle evaluates primitively on the ordered commuting torus.
- **claim-degree-two-restriction** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-degree-two-restriction)): The inclusion of the deck subgroup induces a surjection $$H^2(B\Gamma;\mathbb Z)\longrightarrow H^2(B\mathsf H;\mathbb Z). \label{eq:degree-two-restriction}$$
  - Explanation: A free mapping-cone resolution yields surjectivity, and comparison with bar cochains identifies the map as restriction.
- **claim-graphical-integral-detector** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-torsion-detector)): The graphical group $\Gamma$ is torsion-free. The map $i_\Gamma:\mathbb Z^2\to\Gamma$ sending the ordered basis to $(z,x)$ is injective, and there is a class $c_\Gamma\in H^2(B\Gamma;\mathbb Z)$ such that $$\bigl\langle(Bi_\Gamma)^*c_\Gamma,[T^2]\bigr\rangle=-1. \label{eq:graphical-integral-detector}$$
  - Explanation: Combines torsion-freeness, the embedded torus, and extension of its primitive integral cohomology detector.
- **claim-uniform-trimming** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-assembly-trimming)): For a construction whose constants have already been fixed, $$\\|(1-D_Q)E_{Q,i}\\|^2\le C_\nu2^{-690H_i}, \qquad \delta_\nu:=\\|(1-D)E\\|\le C_\nu2^{-345H_0}.$$ In particular $H_{0,\nu}$ can be chosen so that $\delta_\nu\to0$. If $A=EAE$ and $B=EBE$, and $$\Psi_\nu(A)=UDADU^*,$$ then $$\label{eq:domain-product-error} \\|\Psi_\nu(A)\Psi_\nu(B)-\Psi_\nu(AB)\\| \le\delta_\nu^2\\|A\\|\\|B\\|.$$
  - Explanation: Small carrier traces and finite removed sets bound trimming and the multiplicative defect of compression.
- **claim-active-carrier-synthesis** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-assembly-synthesis)): Let $n_i$ be the number of surviving old base vertices on both sides of height $i$. Then $$\label{eq:raw-synthesis-level} \\|\mathcal J(1-D)E_i\\|^2 \le n_i\sup_Q\\|(1-D_Q)E_{Q,i}\\|^2 \le C_\nu2^{-689H_i}.$$ Consequently $\mathcal J E$ extends to a bounded operator and $$\label{eq:raw-synthesis-sum} \\|\mathcal J E-UDE\\| \le C_\nu\sum_{i\ge0}2^{-344.5H_i} =C_\nu\frac{2^{-344.5H_0}}{1-2^{-344.5b}}.$$
  - Explanation: Incidence multiplicity bounds synthesis on active carriers and makes the heightwise error summable.
- **claim-deleted-incidence-repair** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-deleted-incidence)): There are contractions $\widehat U_{ik}$ on the surviving spaces that send normalized constants exactly to normalized constants in both directions, have centered norm at most $\rho$, and satisfy $$\\|U_{ik}-\widehat U_{ik}\\|\le C\sqrt{\varepsilon_{\mathrm{del}}}.$$ The constant is uniform in the height and the incidence type.
  - Explanation: Repairs compressed incidences to preserve normalized constants exactly while retaining the centered gap.
- **claim-finite-profile-realization** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-finite-profile-realization)): Fix a finite collection of band profiles $f=(f_{ik})_{\|i-k\|\le1}$ of the kind above, a fixed finite matrix size, and a target error $\eta>0$. One can choose the construction parameters so that for each such profile there is a finite matrix group-algebra element $b_f$ satisfying $$\label{eq:finite-profile-realization} \\|b_f-UD\mathcal I f^{\mathrm{mod}}\mathcal I^*DU^*\\|<\eta.$$ The estimate holds simultaneously over all heights and sheets. Profiles may be set to zero on any prescribed parity pattern, on all positive heights, or on the initial height, without a height-dependent enlargement of the alphabet.
  - Explanation: Finite Cayley formulas, constant extraction, trimming, and the residual forest estimate realize prescribed model profiles.
- **claim-source-reduced-membership** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-source-reduced-membership)): The constructions can be chosen so that $\Theta(\mathcal B)\subset\mathcal Q_r$.
  - Explanation: Finite profile meshes with uniform path continuity put the generated source algebra inside the reduced norm quotient.
- **claim-supported-bott-transport** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-supported-bott-rotation)): Let $B$ be a $C^*$-algebra, $e\in M_m(\mathbb C)$ a projection, and $a=a^*\in M_m(B)$ such that $e+a$ is a projection in $M_m(B^+)$. Suppose $v\in B$ is bounded, acts as $v\otimes I_m$ on the matrix factor, and satisfies $v^*va=a$. Then $$[e+a]-[e]=[e+vav^*]-[e]\quad\hbox{in }K_0(B).$$ No contraction assumption on $v$ or identity projection for a carrier is needed.
  - Explanation: An explicit supported projection rotation preserves the Bott difference under bounded scalar-factor transport.
- **claim-eventual-coordinate-equality** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-eventual-coordinate-equality)): Let $B_\nu$ be unital $C^*$-algebras and let $P_\nu,Q_\nu\in M_m(B_\nu)$ be projections in a fixed size. If their classes agree in $K_0((\prod B_\nu)/(\bigoplus B_\nu))$, then $[P_\nu]=[Q_\nu]$ in $K_0(B_\nu)$ for all sufficiently large $\nu$.
  - Explanation: A fixed finite quotient witness lifts to approximate coordinate witnesses, then spectral cutting and polar normalization make them exact.
- **claim-reduced-bott-vanishing** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-reduced-bott-vanishing)): For all sufficiently large $\nu$ in the chosen compatible sequence, the image of the rank-zero Bott generator of $K_0(C_r^*(\langle z,x\rangle))$ is zero integrally in $K_0(C_r^*(\Gamma_\nu))$.
  - Explanation: Transfers quotient Bott cancellation to integral vanishing in each sufficiently late reduced group algebra.
- **claim-finite-subgroup-witness** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-finite-subgroup-witness)): Let $H\le\Gamma$ be finitely generated, and let $P,Q$ be fixed finite matrix projections over $C_r^*(H)$. If their images have equal $K_0$ classes over $C_r^*(\Gamma)$, then some finitely generated subgroup $G_{\mathrm{inj}}$ with $H\le G_{\mathrm{inj}}\le\Gamma$ already has $[P]=[Q]$ in $K_0(C_r^*(G_{\mathrm{inj}}))$.
  - Explanation: Finite polynomial supports of an approximate equality witness generate a subgroup where functional calculus restores the exact equality.

At each surviving slot, separately among its full A source blocks and its full B source blocks, fewer than $.005$ of the updates are unavailable because of a closed gate or a marked or following deterministic identity rule. The last three source gates and the actual prefix gate are open.",
```json
          "source_id": "PM-285.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "For every fixed set of tables, the fraction deleted for gate reasons from either state space at any height is at most $$\\label{eq:gate-budget}\n \\varepsilon_{\\rm gate}=600T2^bD\\,2^{-8s}.$$ On a surviving edge both auxiliary forced choices were unnecessary, so the edge satisfies the universal padded equations <a href=\"#eq:universal-edge-equations\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:universal-edge-equations\">[eq:universal-edge-equations]</a>.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-tower-labels",
        "exact_quote": "For every fixed finite type set and every sufficiently large $s$ as above, the construction has the following properties.*\n\n1.  *The auxiliary state sizes are <a href=\"#eq:state-sizes\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:state-sizes\">[eq:state-sizes]</a>. In a fixed extra slice an incidence has degrees $d,D$. Without slicing, its smaller side acquires the extra factor $2^b$ and its larger side does not. The total degrees are at most $T2^b d$ on A and $T2^bD$ on B.*\n\n\n2.  *The labeling is folded: no two outgoing oriented edges at a vertex have the same label. Its signed alphabet satisfies $$\\label{eq:sharp-alphabet}\n     |\\mathcal S|\\le 2TM2^b d2^r,\n     \\qquad\n     \\frac{\\sqrt{|\\mathcal S|}}q\n     \\le 2^{-.000025s+O(\\log s)+O_{T,b}(1)}\\longrightarrow0.$$*\n\n3.  *At every height the fraction of removed vertices on each side is at most $\\varepsilon_{\\rm gate}+\\varepsilon_{\\rm sep}$. This bound can be made arbitrarily small, uniformly over heights and all table choices. The separate-parity and terminal gate conclusions of Lemma <a href=\"#lem:gate-deletions\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:gate-deletions\">4</a> hold.*\n\n4.  *Every immersed path in $X^{\\rm good}$ starting at height $i$ and having length at least $\\lceil1.002m_i\\rceil$ is the unique immersed path with that oriented word anywhere in $X^{\\rm good}$. This is a global assertion over all heights and components.",
        "source_id": "PM-285.1"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-conditional-fourier",
        "exact_quote": "Conditional on data $C$, let $U\\in\\mathbb F_2^t$ and $V\\in\\mathbb F_2^w$ be independent uniform vectors, independent also of auxiliary data $W$. For $a$ in a set of $q_0$ slots let $K_a:\\mathbb F_2^t\\to\\mathbb F_2^w$ be linear, let $o_a=o_a(C,W)$, and let $f_a(C,z)$ have no separate dependence on $U,V,W$. Suppose every class of slots with a common value of $\\zeta K_a$, for any nonzero functional $\\zeta\\in(\\mathbb F_2^w)^*$, has at most $\\theta q_0$ members. Set $$F=\\frac1{q_0}\\sum_a f_a(C,V+K_aU+o_a),\\qquad\n \\mu(C)=\\frac1{q_0}\\sum_a2^{-w}\\sum_z f_a(C,z).$$ Then $\\mathbb E(F\\mid C,W,U)=\\mu(C)$ and $$\\label{eq:fourier-conditional-bound}\n \\mathbb E|F-\\mu(C)|^2\n \\leq\\theta\\,\\mathbb E\\left[\\frac1{q_0}\\sum_a2^{-w}\n                       \\sum_z|f_a(C,z)|^2\\right].$$",
        "source_id": "PM-285.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-conditional-fourier",
          "exact_quote": "Conditional on data $C$, let $U\\in\\mathbb F_2^t$ and $V\\in\\mathbb F_2^w$ be independent uniform vectors, independent also of auxiliary data $W$. Suppose every class of slots with a common value of $\\zeta K_a$, for any nonzero functional $\\zeta\\in(\\mathbb F_2^w)^*$, has at most $\\theta q_0$ members. Set $$F=\\frac1{q_0}\\sum_a f_a(C,V+K_aU+o_a),\\qquad\n \\mu(C)=\\frac1{q_0}\\sum_a2^{-w}\\sum_z f_a(C,z).$$ Then $\\mathbb E(F\\mid C,W,U)=\\mu(C)$ and $$\\label{eq:fourier-conditional-bound}\n \\mathbb E|F-\\mu(C)|^2\n \\leq\\theta\\,\\mathbb E\\left[\\frac1{q_0}\\sum_a2^{-w}\n                       \\sum_z|f_a(C,z)|^2\\right].$$",
          "source_id": "PM-285.1"
        }

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-pruning-reconstruction",
        "exact_quote": "Fix a coherent-path pattern with different junction coefficients, and fix its free endpoint u-bits.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-pruning-rank-process",
        "exact_quote": "Suppose that, at each of finitely many source blocks, a test is selected from $\\mathcal F_{<t}$, and its product-law probability is at most $2^{-R_t}$. The nonnegative integers $R_t$ and $k_t$ are $\\mathcal F_{<t}$-measurable. If an event $A$ implies that all tests pass, that $\\sum_tR_t\\geq R$, and that $\\sum_tk_t\\leq K$, then $$\\mathbb P(A)\\leq \\exp(\\epsilon_sK)2^{-R}.$$ The event $A$ may additionally require later minimality, missed-gate counts, and full consistency of the reconstructed vertex states.",
        "source_id": "PM-285.1"
      },

        "exact_quote": "If both incident common cutoffs equal the receiving vertex’s own cutoff, the terminal constraints from two independent incident evaluations have product-law probability exactly $2^{-\\lambda(v)}$.         "anchor": "stmt-lem-heisenberg-law",
        "exact_quote": "Let $W$ be a nonempty closed edge path in $X$, reduced as a based edge path: no two consecutive traversals in its linear ordering are mutually inverse. Suppose the word of $W$ is a $\\mathsf H$-law. Then its support contains a bicycle $B$ such that every edge of $B$ is traversed at least four times by $W$.       },

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-pruning",
        "exact_quote": "Given any $\\varepsilon>0$, the allowed parameters and the universal tables can be chosen so that the additional two deletions in this Section remove less than an $\\varepsilon$ fraction of each $\\mathscr A_i$ and $\\mathscr B_i$. In the surviving induced old graph $X_{\\mathrm{old}}$:*\n\n1.  *the folded labeling, padded-prefix recovery of Lemma <a href=\"#lem:tower-recovery\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:tower-recovery\">5</a>, and global word uniqueness of Proposition <a href=\"#prop:tower-labels\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:tower-labels\">7</a> remain valid;*\n\n2.  *a nonempty cyclically reduced closed path based at height $i$ has length greater than $.93m_i$;*\n\n3.  *every bicycle containing a vertex of height $i$ has length at least $1.52m_i$;*\n\n4.  *every nonempty cyclically reduced closed path based at height $i$ with length at most $6.04m_i$ is not a law in the independent edge variables of $\\mathsf H$.*\n\n*The expansion conclusions for the undeleted incidences hold simultaneously, and the degrees remain fixed along the tower.",
        "source_id": "PM-285.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-pruning",
          "exact_quote": "Given any $\\varepsilon>0$, the allowed parameters and the universal tables can be chosen so that the additional two deletions in this Section remove less than an $\\varepsilon$ fraction of each $\\mathscr A_i$ and $\\mathscr B_i$. In the surviving induced old graph $X_{\\mathrm{old}}$:*\n\n1.  *the folded labeling, padded-prefix recovery of Lemma <a href=\"#lem:tower-recovery\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:tower-recovery\">5</a>, and global word uniqueness of Proposition <a href=\"#prop:tower-labels\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:tower-labels\">7</a> remain valid;*\n\n2.  *a nonempty cyclically reduced closed path based at height $i$ has length greater than $.93m_i$;*\n\n3.  *every bicycle containing a vertex of height $i$ has length at least $1.52m_i$;*\n\n4.  *every nonempty cyclically reduced closed path based at height $i$ with length at most $6.04m_i$ is not a law in the independent edge variables of $\\mathsf H$.*\n\n*The expansion conclusions for the undeleted incidences hold simultaneously, and the degrees remain fixed along the tower.",
          "source_id": "PM-285.1"
        }

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-supported-spreading",
        "exact_quote": "For sufficiently large initial height, the family of projections $$\\lambda(y)^jE_i\\lambda(y)^{-j},\\qquad \\left\\lvert j\\right\\rvert\\leq J_i,$$ is pairwise orthogonal. Consequently $$E_iT_i^*T_iE_i=E_i,\\qquad P_i=T_iE_iT_i^*$$ is a projection, and $$\\label{eq:spread-trace}\n \\tau_{\\mathsf H}(P_i)=\\tau_{\\mathsf H}(E_i)\n \\leq C_R2^{-700H_i}.$$ If $f$ is supported in both enlarged rectangles at heights $i$ and $k$, and $g$ in both enlarged rectangles at heights $k$ and $j$, then $$(T_i fT_k^*)(T_k gT_j^*)=T_i(fg)T_j^*,\\qquad\n (T_i fT_k^*)^*=T_k f^*T_i^*,\\qquad\n \\left\\lVert T_i fT_k^*\\right\\rVert\\leq\\left\\lVert f\\right\\rVert.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-285.1"
      },
      "evidence": [

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-sampled-symbols",
        "exact_quote": "Fix b, a finite matrix dimension, a positive accuracy $\\eta$, and a finite list of compact smooth symbol profiles and height selections as above. The list may include cutoffs, both parities, the initial-height selection, and any finite mesh of the adjacent rescaling and rotation families. Allocate a prescribed incidence type for every required same or adjacent layer symbol. For sufficiently large s and then sufficiently large initial height, the surviving base graph admits a voltage on every edge and finite-net matrix weights such that the following hold simultaneously.*\n\n1.  *The voltage cover has no nontrivial cyclically reduced closed walk of length at most $6.03m_i$ based at an old vertex of height i.*\n\n2.  *All weighted blocks may use the common denominator q. If $\\mathcal V_{\\tau,ik}$ is that normalized weighted convolution block and the prescribed symbol is $S_{\\tau,ik}=T_if_{\\tau,ik}T_k^*$, then $$\\left\\lVert\\mathcal V_{\\tau,ik}\n               -U_{\\tau,ik}\\otimes S_{\\tau,ik}\\right\\rVert\\leq\\eta$$ in the full regular operator norm, at every height. The tensor notation here means that each scalar incidence entry multiplies the same matrix-valued deck convolution symbol.*\n\n3.  *Weights are read from one finite set, independent of height. Appending their net indices to the existing edge labels preserves the labelled-graph uniqueness properties and gives $$\\log_2\\left\\lvert\\mathcal A\\right\\rvert\n     \\leq1.00005s+O(\\log s)+O_{b,\\eta,\\mathrm{profiles}}(1)\n     <\\log_2(q^2)$$ for sufficiently large s. In particular $\\sqrt{\\left\\lvert\\mathcal A\\right\\rvert}/q$ tends to zero as s increases after the fixed data. Zero-weight edges are retained in the voltage cover.",
        "source_id": "PM-285.1"
      },

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-fourier-envelope",
        "exact_quote": "Let $\\left\\lvert i-k\\right\\rvert\\leq1$, and let $f$ belong to the profile family <a href=\"#eq:profile-family\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:profile-family\">[eq:profile-family]</a>, expressed at reference height $i$. Write $$T_i fT_k^*=\\sum_{n,l,c}A_{n,l,c}\\lambda(x^ny^lz^c),\n \\qquad N=N_i,\\quad Z=Z_i,\\quad J=J_i.$$ For every pair of nonnegative integers $A,P$, with $P\\geq3$, $$\\label{eq:weighted-central-envelope}\n \\sum_c(1+\\left\\lvert c\\right\\rvert/Z)^A\\left\\lVert A_{n,l,c}\\right\\rVert\n \\leq C_{A,P}N^{-1}(1+\\left\\lvert n\\right\\rvert/N)^{-P}\n       (1+J\\left\\lVert n\\alpha\\right\\rVert_{\\mathbb R/\\mathbb Z})^{-3},\n \\qquad \\left\\lvert l\\right\\rvert\\leq C_bJ,$$ and the coefficients vanish for larger $\\left\\lvert l\\right\\rvert$. The constants are uniform in height and in the compact profile family. In particular these symbols have a uniformly bounded Fourier one-norm. For each $\\eta>0$ they can be truncated, with one-norm error at most $\\eta$, to $$\\label{eq:fourier-box}\n \\left\\lvert n\\right\\rvert\\leq L_\\eta N,\\qquad \\left\\lvert l\\right\\rvert\\leq C_bJ,\\qquad\n \\left\\lvert c\\right\\rvert\\leq L_\\eta Z,$$ where $L_\\eta$ is independent of height.",
        "source_id": "PM-285.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-fourier-envelope",
          "exact_quote": "Let $\\left\\lvert i-k\\right\\rvert\\leq1$, and let $f$ belong to the profile family <a href=\"#eq:profile-family\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:profile-family\">[eq:profile-family]</a>, expressed at reference height $i$. Write $$T_i fT_k^*=\\sum_{n,l,c}A_{n,l,c}\\lambda(x^ny^lz^c),\n \\qquad N=N_i,\\quad Z=Z_i,\\quad J=J_i.$$ For every pair of nonnegative integers $A,P$, with $P\\geq3$, $$\\label{eq:weighted-central-envelope}\n \\sum_c(1+\\left\\lvert c\\right\\rvert/Z)^A\\left\\lVert A_{n,l,c}\\right\\rVert\n \\leq C_{A,P}N^{-1}(1+\\left\\lvert n\\right\\rvert/N)^{-P}\n       (1+J\\left\\lVert n\\alpha\\right\\rVert_{\\mathbb R/\\mathbb Z})^{-3},\n \\qquad \\left\\lvert l\\right\\rvert\\leq C_bJ,$$ and the coefficients vanish for larger $\\left\\lvert l\\right\\rvert$. The constants are uniform in height and in the compact profile family. In particular these symbols have a uniformly bounded Fourier one-norm. For each $\\eta>0$ they can be truncated, with one-norm error at most $\\eta$, to $$\\label{eq:fourier-box}\n \\left\\lvert n\\right\\rvert\\leq L_\\eta N,\\qquad \\left\\lvert l\\right\\rvert\\leq C_bJ,\\qquad\n \\left\\lvert c\\right\\rvert\\leq L_\\eta Z,$$ where $L_\\eta$ is independent of height.",
          "source_id": "PM-285.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $\\left\\lvert i-k\\right\\rvert\\leq1$, and let $f$ belong to the profile family <a href=\"#eq:profile-family\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:profile-family\">[eq:profile-family]</a>, expressed at reference height $i$. Write $$T_i fT_k^*=\\sum_{n,l,c}A_{n,l,c}\\lambda(x^ny^lz^c),\n \\qquad N=N_i,\\quad Z=Z_i,\\quad J=J_i.$$ For every pair of nonnegative integers $A,P$, with $P\\geq3$, $$\\label{eq:weighted-central-envelope}\n \\sum_c(1+\\left\\lvert c\\right\\rvert/Z)^A\\left\\lVert A_{n,l,c}\\right\\rVert\n \\leq C_{A,P}N^{-1}(1+\\left\\lvert n\\right\\rvert/N)^{-P}\n       (1+J\\left\\lVert n\\alpha\\right\\rVert_{\\mathbb R/\\mathbb Z})^{-3},\n \\qquad \\left\\lvert l\\right\\rvert\\leq C_bJ,$$ and the coefficients vanish for larger $\\left\\lvert l\\right\\rvert$. The constants are uniform in height and in the compact profile family. In particular these symbols have a uniformly bounded Fourier one-norm. For each $\\eta>0$ they can be truncated, with one-norm error at most $\\eta$, to $$\\label{eq:fourier-box}\n \\left\\lvert n\\right\\rvert\\leq L_\\eta N,\\qquad \\left\\lvert l\\right\\rvert\\leq C_bJ,\\qquad\n \\left\\lvert c\\right\\rvert\\leq L_\\eta Z,$$ where $L_\\eta$ is independent of height.",
      "explanation": "Separates horizontal frequency decay from central tails and yields a uniform Fourier mass bound and finite truncations.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-product-sampler",
        "exact_quote": "For every fixed $\\eta>0$, each symbol in Lemma <a href=\"#lem:fourier-envelope\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:fourier-envelope\">30</a> has a finite distribution of pairs $(W,v)$, with $W\\in M_\\ell(\\mathbb C)$ and $v=x^ny^lz^c\\in\\mathsf H$, satisfying $$\\left\\lVert W\\right\\rVert\\leq M_\\eta,\\qquad\n \\left\\lVert\\mathbb E[W\\lambda(v)]-T_ifT_k^*\\right\\rVert_1\\leq\\eta.$$ The horizontal coordinates n and l are independent. Their maximal atoms satisfy $$\\label{eq:horizontal-atoms}\n \\sup_n\\Pr(n)=O_b(J_i/N_i),\\qquad\n \\sup_l\\Pr(l)=O_b(J_i^{-1}).$$ All possible group coordinates, including those of inverses, are bounded by $2^{a_0H_i}$ for a fixed exponent $a_0$, for example $a_0=502$, after increasing the initial height. The weights can additionally be restricted to one finite adjoint-closed net, at the expense of any prescribed further one-norm error. All constants and the net are independent of height.",

Their maximal atoms satisfy $$\\label{eq:horizontal-atoms}\n \\sup_n\\Pr(n)=O_b(J_i/N_i),\\qquad\n \\sup_l\\Pr(l)=O_b(J_i^{-1}).$$ All possible group coordinates, including those of inverses, are bounded by $2^{a_0H_i}$ for a fixed exponent $a_0$, for example $a_0=502$, after increasing the initial height. The weights can additionally be restricted to one finite adjoint-closed net, at the expense of any prescribed further one-norm error. All constants and the net are independent of height.",

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-polynomial-avoidance",
        "exact_quote": "Use independent edge draws as in Lemma <a href=\"#lem:product-sampler\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:product-sampler\">31</a>. For a fixed base walk, reuse its one voltage variable on every occurrence of an unoriented edge and use its inverse in the reverse direction. If the associated word is not a law in $\\mathsf H$, the probability that its holonomy is the identity is at most $$C_b2^{-50H_{\\min}},$$ where $H_{\\min}$ is the smallest reference height of an edge in the walk.         "exact_quote": "Suppose a finite set of group elements and their inverses have coordinates bounded by $2^{a_0H}$, with $H\\geq1$.         "exact_quote": "Fix a surviving incidence type and one of its same or adjacent layer blocks.         "anchor": "stmt-lem-private-completion",
        "exact_quote": "There is a connected, countable, folded labeled graph $X$ containing $X_{\\mathrm o}$ as its old subgraph, and an extension of the voltages, with the following properties.*\n\n1.  *Each added edge has its own new positive letter, used on no other base edge. Every new vertex has degree two and has only private incident edges. The maximum degree is at most $K_{b,\\epsilon}D+6$.*\n\n2.  *The function $m$ extends to the new vertices, remains at least $m_{\\min}$, and satisfies $|m(v)-m(w)|\\le a$ on every edge.*\n\n\n3.  *The voltage cover $\\widetilde X$, with edges $$\\label{eq:voltage-convention}\n     (v,h)\\longrightarrow(w,hh_e)$$ over $e\\colon v\\to w$, is connected. Its deck action is the left action $f(v,h)=(v,fh)$ of $\\mathsf H$.*\n\n4.  *Every simple cycle $C$ of $\\widetilde X$ satisfies $|C|>6.03m(v)$ at every vertex $v$ of $C$.*\n\n5.  *If two paths in $\\widetilde X$ have the same word and their projected occurrences in $X$ differ, neither contains a private edge. If they are immersed and start at $v,w$, their common length is less than both $Lm(v)$ and $Lm(w)$.*\n\n*The scale on the cover is the pullback of the scale on $X$.",

      },

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-word-diagram",
        "exact_quote": "If a finite word $w$ is trivial in <a href=\"#eq:graphical-presentation\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:graphical-presentation\">[eq:graphical-presentation]</a>, there is a finite plane multigraph diagram with exterior contour reading the literal word $w$. More precisely, if in the free group on $S$ $$w=\\prod_{j=1}^n u_jr_ju_j^{-1},$$ where each $r_j$ is read on a chosen closed source walk, a diagram may be chosen with filling cost at most $\\sum_j|r_j|$. Conversely, the exterior word of any such diagram is a product of conjugates of its bounded-face words in the free group, using each bounded facial word once with the compatible orientation. Its labeled one-skeleton therefore has a well-defined vertex map to the formal Cayley graph after prescribing the image of one vertex.",
        "source_id": "PM-285.1"
      },
      "evidence": [

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-graphical-realization",
        "exact_quote": "There is an injective map of vertices and formal oriented edges $$j\\colon\\widetilde X\\longrightarrow\\operatorname{Cay}(\\Gamma,S)$$ preserving labels and sending $(v_*,1)$ to the identity. The subgroup of vertices over $v_*$ defines an injective homomorphism $\\phi\\colon\\mathsf H\\hookrightarrow\\Gamma$. If $t_v=j(v,1)$, then $$\\label{eq:deck-transport}\n j(v,h)=\\phi(h)t_v.$$ The full group of label-preserving automorphisms of $\\widetilde X$ is its deck group. Consequently the distinct translated images, called *sheets*, are indexed by $\\Gamma/\\phi(\\mathsf H)$, and the stabilizer of $g j(\\widetilde X)$ under left translation is $g\\phi(\\mathsf H)g^{-1}$.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-sheet-polygon",
        "exact_quote": "Let $Q_1,\\ldots,Q_k$ be distinct sheets, $k\\ge2$, and let $g_i\\in Q_i\\cap Q_{i+1}$, with indices modulo $k$. Contacts may coincide. Choose a source path in $Q_i$ from $g_{i-1}$ to $g_i$ for each $i$. There is a finite diagram and a choice of these paths, *with every prescribed endpoint $g_i$ fixed*, such that:*\n\n1.  *Each side is linearly immersed. After retaining all junction vertices and suppressing other degree-two vertices, every arc has two different projected occurrences in $X$. Each occurrence is immersed, contains no private edge, and has length less than $Lm_Q(v)$ at its initial point. Each entire nonconstant side is the concatenation of its exterior arc occurrences.*\n\n2.  *Every bounded face has at least seven arc incidences.*\n\n3.  *If $B$ counts exterior arc incidences, counting an exterior bridge twice, then $$\\label{eq:polygon-exterior}\n     B\\le4k-6.$$*\n\n*A diagram with no edges has $B=0$ and all its sides constant. In particular, a nonconstant side cannot have a private vertex as an endpoint. A side whose intrinsic endpoint distance is greater than $3.014m_Q(v)$ at its initial endpoint has at least four arc incidences.",
        "source_id": "PM-285.1"
      },

          "anchor": "stmt-lem-sheet-polygon",
          "exact_quote": "Let $Q_1,\\ldots,Q_k$ be distinct sheets, $k\\ge2$, and let $g_i\\in Q_i\\cap Q_{i+1}$, with indices modulo $k$. Contacts may coincide. Choose a source path in $Q_i$ from $g_{i-1}$ to $g_i$ for each $i$. There is a finite diagram and a choice of these paths, *with every prescribed endpoint $g_i$ fixed*, such that:*\n\n1.  *Each side is linearly immersed. After retaining all junction vertices and suppressing other degree-two vertices, every arc has two different projected occurrences in $X$. Each occurrence is immersed, contains no private edge, and has length less than $Lm_Q(v)$ at its initial point. Each entire nonconstant side is the concatenation of its exterior arc occurrences.*\n\n2.  *Every bounded face has at least seven arc incidences.*\n\n3.  *If $B$ counts exterior arc incidences, counting an exterior bridge twice, then $$\\label{eq:polygon-exterior}\n     B\\le4k-6.$$*\n\n*A diagram with no edges has $B=0$ and all its sides constant. In particular, a nonconstant side cannot have a private vertex as an endpoint. A side whose intrinsic endpoint distance is greater than $3.014m_Q(v)$ at its initial endpoint has at least four arc incidences.",
          "source_id": "PM-285.1"
        }

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-allowed-domain-sets",
        "exact_quote": "For an old vertex $v\\in Q$, set $$B_Q(v)=\\{w\\in V(Q):d_Q(v,w)\\leq\\lfloor\\kappa m_Q(v)\\rfloor\\}.$$ The graphs $\\operatorname{Star}_Q(B_Q(v))$ and $\\operatorname{Star}_Q(\\{w\\})$, for a private vertex $w$, are forests.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-sheet-peeling",
        "exact_quote": "Every nonempty finite family $\\mathcal F$ has a sheet $Q$ for which $I_{\\mathcal F}(Q)$ is either empty, contained in $B_Q(v)$ for some old contact $v$, or equal to a singleton private contact.         "source_id": "PM-285.1"
      },

        "exact_quote": "There are sets $R_Q\\subseteq V(Q)$, one for each sheet, such that each $R_Q$ is empty, an old-centered ball $B_Q(v)$, or a private singleton, and the sets $D_Q=V(Q)\\setminus R_Q$ have the following properties.*\n\n1.  *The physical vertex sets $D_Q$ are pairwise disjoint.*\n\n2.  *Put $$E_{\\mathrm{int}}=\n     \\bigcup_{Q\\in\\mathcal Q}\n     \\{e\\in E(Q):\\text{both endpoints of }e\\text{ lie in }D_Q\\}.$$ Every edge in this union has a unique such sheet, and $(V(\\mathcal C),E(\\mathcal C)\\setminus E_{\\mathrm{int}})$ is a forest.*\n\n3.  *$\\operatorname{Star}_Q(R_Q)$ is a forest.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-sheet-incidences",
        "exact_quote": "The map sending a sheet incidence to its physical vertex and its base type is a bijection $$\\{(Q,u):Q\\in\\mathcal Q,\\ u\\in V(Q)\\}\n       \\longrightarrow \\Gamma\\times V(X).\n \\label{eq:sheet-incidence-bijection}$$ In particular, a physical vertex occurs in at most $n_i^{\\mathrm{full}}=\\left\\lvert\\mathscr A_i\\right\\rvert+\\left\\lvert\\mathscr B_i\\right\\rvert$ old layer-$i$ incidences, and $$n_i^{\\mathrm{full}}=(1+2^{2r-s})2^{H_i}\\leq C_s2^{H_i}.\n \\label{eq:old-layer-incidences}$$",
        "source_id": "PM-285.1"
      },

        }

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-free-resolution-comparison",
        "exact_quote": "Let $P_\\bullet\\to M$ and $Q_\\bullet\\to N$ be exact augmented complexes of free modules over a ring $A$. Every homomorphism $M\\to N$ lifts to a chain map $P_\\bullet\\to Q_\\bullet$. Any two such lifts are chain homotopic. In particular, two free resolutions of one module are chain homotopy equivalent, and remain so after tensoring with a right $A$-module or applying $\\operatorname{Hom}_A(-,L)$.",

      },

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-finite-subgroup-witness",
        "exact_quote": "Let $H\\le\\Gamma$ be finitely generated, and let $P,Q$ be fixed finite matrix projections over $C_r^*(H)$. <a id="pm-s2"></a>
```
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **coefficient-free reduced assembly map** ([source](#sec:introduction)): For a torsion-free discrete group $G_{\mathrm{inj}}$, its coefficient-free form is $$\mu_{G_{\mathrm{inj}}}^r\colon K_*(BG_{\mathrm{inj}})\longrightarrow K_*(C_r^*(G_{\mathrm{inj}})).$$
- **compactly supported topological K-homology** ([source](#sec:introduction)): Here $BG_{\mathrm{inj}}$ is a classifying space, $K_*(BG_{\mathrm{inj}})$ is complex topological $K$-homology with compact support, namely the directed limit over finite subcomplexes, and $C_r^*(G_{\mathrm{inj}})$ is the reduced group $C^*$-algebra.
- **slot** ([source](#sec:labeling)): A *slot* is a named candidate incidence at a vertex.
- **voltage assignment** ([source](#pm-h-5)): A voltage assigns a group element $h_e$ to an oriented base edge $v\to w$; its lift sends $(v,f)$ to $(w,fh_e)$, and the reverse edge uses $h_e^{-1}$.
- **immersed path** ([source](#pm-h-13)): A path is immersed if it does not immediately traverse an edge and then its reverse.
- **path radius** ([source](#pm-h-13)): The radius of a position on a path is its smaller distance in edges to the two endpoints.
- **coherent return** ([source](#stmt-def-coherent-return)): In band $k$, a coherent return is a positive-length nonbacktracking path $$v_0,e_1,v_1,\ldots,e_p,v_p$$ in $X^{\mathrm{good}}$, entirely in that band, whose endpoints are on the same side and agree through an available aligned depth $$\label{eq:coherent-threshold} h\geq ps/2+M_k, \qquad h\leq\min\{D(v_0),D(v_p)\}.$$ It is minimal if no proper positive-length contiguous subpath is a coherent return for the same band and the same $M_k$. An exact closed nonbacktracking path is included whenever it satisfies the available-depth condition.
- **bicycle** ([source](#sec:pruning)): A bicycle is a finite connected subgraph with first Betti number two and with no vertex of valence one.
- **Heisenberg law** ([source](#pm-h-31)): Its word is an *$\mathsf H$-law* if it evaluates to the identity for every independent assignment $t_e\in\mathsf H$.
- **folded labeling** ([source](#pm-h-44)): A labeling is *folded* if, at each vertex, each signed formal letter is read on at most one outgoing edge incidence.
- **filling cost** ([source](#pm-h-46)): The *filling cost* is the sum of the perimeters of the bounded faces.
- **sheet** ([source](#stmt-prop-graphical-realization)): Consequently the distinct translated images, called *sheets*, are indexed by $\Gamma/\phi(\mathsf H)$, and the stabilizer of $g j(\widetilde X)$ under left translation is $g\phi(\mathsf H)g^{-1}$.
- **edge-incidence neighborhood** ([source](#pm-h-50)): For a vertex set $B\subseteq V(Q)$, let $\operatorname{Star}_Q(B)$ denote the subgraph consisting of all edges of $Q$ with at least one endpoint in $B$, together with their endpoints.
- **cycle module** ([source](#pm-h-51)): The cycle module of a graph $Y$ is $Z_1(Y;R)=\ker(\partial:C_1(Y;R)\to C_0(Y;R))$.
- **norm sequence quotient** ([source](#pm-h-61)): Define $$\mathcal Q_r=\frac{\prod_\nu A_\nu}{\bigoplus_\nu A_\nu}, \qquad \mathcal Q_{\mathrm{op}} =\frac{\prod_\nu B(\mathscr L_\nu)}{\bigoplus_\nu B(\mathscr L_\nu)}.$$
- **exact source algebra** ([source](#pm-h-61)): Define the exact source algebra $\mathcal B$ to be the $C^*$-subalgebra of $\mathcal M$ generated by the scalar matrix entries of these four compact differences and of the two paths just defined.

|---|---|---|---|---|
| q-incidence-small-degree | d | = | $2^{.40005s}$ | Fixed along the tower height; may change with accuracy. |
| q-incidence-large-degree | D | = | $2^{.60005s}$ | Fixed along the tower height; may change with accuracy. |
| q-incidence-normalizer | q | = | $2^{.50005s}$ | The normalizer is independent of tower height. |
| q-height-slope | a | ≤ | $10^{-6}$ | Imposed by increasing the block parameter. |
| q-gate-deletion-budget | fraction deleted for gate reasons | ≤ | $600T2^bD\,2^{-8s}$ | Uniform over heights and table choices. |
| q-separation-deletion-budget | fraction deleted for separation | ≤ | $2\left( \frac{2^{-Mb/2}}{1-2^{-Mb/2}} +\frac{2^{-\eta_{\rm sep} H_0}}{1-2^{-\eta_{\rm sep} b}} \right).$ | Uniform in the larger height. |
| q-global-word-uniqueness-threshold | immersed path length sufficient for global word uniqueness | ≥ | $\lceil1.002m_i\rceil$ | Applies globally across heights and components. |
| q-centered-mean-norm | centered conditional mean incidence norm | ≤ | $\sqrt{5\theta}$ | Uniform in history, cutoff, slice, and adjacent-height incidence. |
| q-centered-incidence-gap | centered singular norm of an auxiliary incidence | ≤ | $1-h_0^2/2$ | The spectral bound is fixed independently of tower height. |
| q-predictable-test-probability | \mathbb P(A) | ≤ | $\exp(\epsilon_sK)2^{-R}$ | Accumulated rank and exposed-table budgets. |
| q-return-count | expected number of rooted minimal coherent returns | ≤ | $2^{.975H_i}$ | Either side at the specified height, over all eligible bands. |
| q-old-base-girth | length of a nonempty cyclically reduced closed path | > | $.93m_i$ | Paths based at the specified height. |
| q-bicycle-count | expected number of rooted embedded short bicycles | ≤ | $2^{.89H_i}$ | Either side at the specified height; bicycles below the stated length cutoff. |
| q-heisenberg-law-length | \ell(W) | ≥ | $4\ell(B)$ | The bicycle lies in the path support and all its edges have the specified traversal multiplicity. |
| q-spread-carrier-trace | \tau_{\mathsf H}(P_i) | ≤ | $C_R2^{-700H_i}$ | The constant depends on the support rectangle and is uniform in height. |
| q-holonomy-identity-probability | probability of identity holonomy | ≤ | $C_b2^{-50H_{\min}}$ | Controlled by the minimum edge reference scale; zero matrix weights do not remove the voltage condition. |
| q-support-cycle-rank | \dim H_1(S;\mathbb Q) | ≤ | $\max\{1,\lceil20K\rceil\}^2$ | Depends on the ratio of total length to the strict girth threshold. |
| q-repeated-edge-walk-count | number of rooted closed walks without singleton edges | ≤ | $[72(2R+2)^2\Delta]^h$ | A fixed root, walk length, degree bound, and support-rank bound. |
| q-centered-block-tail | probability that centered block norm exceeds its tolerance | ≤ | $2^{-6H_i}$ | Uniform in height after the prescribed parameter choice order. |
| q-polygon-exterior-budget | B | ≤ | $4k-6$ | Counts exterior arc incidences with bridge multiplicity. |
| q-removed-ball-cardinality | \left\lvert B_Q(v)\right\rvert | ≤ | $2^{10H_i}$ | The bound uses any old layer met by the ball. |
| q-integral-detector-pairing | \bigl\langle(Bi_\Gamma)^*c_\Gamma,[T^2]\bigr\rangle | = | $-1$ | The ordered generators are the central generator followed by the horizontal generator. |
| q-global-trimming | \delta_\nu | ≤ | $C_\nu2^{-345H_0}$ | The constant is fixed before increasing the initial height. |
| q-deleted-incidence-perturbation | \\|U_{ik}-\widehat U_{ik}\\| | ≤ | $C\sqrt{\varepsilon_{\mathrm{del}}}$ | Uniform in height and incidence type; repaired operators preserve normalized constants. |
