<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-coefficient-pools** [claim](#stmt-lem-coefficient-pools) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-coefficient-pools): Uniform injections and a without-replacement exponential tail bound. -> Distinct coefficients with bounded restricted-functional multiplicity.
- **step-incidence-inversion** [claim](#stmt-lem-incidence-inversion) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-incidence-inversion): Support preservation and triangular inversion from either endpoint. -> Unique slot edges and independent uniform raw coordinates.
- **step-gate-budget** [claim](#stmt-lem-gate-deletions) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-gate-deletions): Uniform raw bits, Markov bounds, and a union over slots. -> Small gate deletion budget and universal surviving equations.
- **step-prefix-recovery** [claim](#stmt-lem-tower-recovery) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-tower-recovery): Induction through individual interleaved blocks using distinct affine evaluations. -> Agreement of padded prefixes from common immersed words.
- **step-separation-budget** [claim](#stmt-lem-separation-deletions) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-separation-deletions): Sum zero-prefix probabilities over smaller heights. -> A uniform separation deletion bound.
- **step-deterministic-tower** [claim](#stmt-prop-tower-labels) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-tower-labels): Folded slot inversion, sharp alphabet counting, and midpoint recovery with height separation. -> The folded tower with global long-word uniqueness and alphabet margin.
- **step-fresh-matchings** [claim](#stmt-lem-fresh-matching-coordinates) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-fresh-matching-coordinates): Identify current prefix tables as complete slot-fiber bijections. -> Independent current perfect matchings unaffected by future tables.
- **step-conditional-fourier** [claim](#stmt-lem-conditional-fourier) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-conditional-fourier): Character orthogonality, multiplicity grouping, and Parseval. -> A conditional mean identity and variance bound.
- **step-conditional-mean** [claim](#stmt-lem-conditional-mean) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-conditional-mean): Orthogonal prefix-band decomposition and terminal-prefix averaging. -> Uniform centered norm bound for the conditional mean incidence.
- **step-sequential-law** [claim](#stmt-prop-sequential-law) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-sequential-law): Cut concentration, finite local-lemma avoidance, Cheeger bounds, and successive conditional kernels. -> A tower law with simultaneous expansion and controlled joint current tests.
- **step-coherent-junction** [claim](#stmt-lem-coherent-junction) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-coherent-junction): Trim equal endpoint labels and contradict minimality or foldedness. -> Different coefficient names at a minimal return junction.
- **step-pattern-reconstruction** [claim](#stmt-lem-pruning-reconstruction) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-pruning-reconstruction): Formal full-block reconstruction with fixed endpoint raw inputs. -> Unique candidate states, predictable queries, and a free-coordinate bound.
- **step-rank-process** [claim](#stmt-lem-pruning-rank-process) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-pruning-rank-process): Multiply predictable success factors into a nonnegative process of nonincreasing expectation. -> A joint passing-probability bound allowing later validity predicates.
- **step-terminal-codimension** [claim](#stmt-lem-pruning-terminal-codimension) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-pruning-terminal-codimension): Affine image dimension counts on actual active random prefixes. -> Equal-cutoff compatibility rank and conservative adjacent-cutoff loss.
- **step-key-prefixes** [claim](#stmt-lem-pruning-key-prefix) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-pruning-key-prefix): Read the earlier receiver data forced by equality of complete table keys. -> Available receiver-prefix agreement from repeated keys.
- **step-end-guard** [claim](#stmt-lem-pruning-end-guard) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-pruning-end-guard): Use repeated-key prefix agreement to find a shorter coherent contiguous subpath. -> Distinct terminal keys outside the guards.
- **step-return-count** [claim](#stmt-lem-pruning-return-count) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-pruning-return-count): Sum label patterns and free coordinates against predictable terminal rank. -> Expected coherent-return count below the layer scale.
- **step-base-girth** [claim](#stmt-lem-pruning-base-girth) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-pruning-base-girth): Show a short closed path satisfies the coherent-return depth threshold. -> Strict base-girth bound after the initial deletion.
- **step-theta-keys** [claim](#stmt-lem-pruning-theta-keys) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-pruning-theta-keys): Turn shared terminal or late branch keys into forbidden coherent paths inside a theta. -> Terminal key distinctness and late branch-key separation.
- **step-triple-ranks** [claim](#stmt-lem-pruning-triple-ranks) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-pruning-triple-ranks): Solve branch relations using independent table values and exclusive keys. -> Product-law ranks for branch constraints.
- **step-theta-count** [claim](#stmt-lem-pruning-theta-count) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-pruning-theta-count): Combine disjoint chronological terminal and branch rank tests with pattern enumeration. -> Expected short embedded bicycle count below the layer scale.
- **step-heisenberg-laws** [claim](#stmt-lem-heisenberg-law) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-heisenberg-law): Balance edge exponents, analyze paired intervals, and extract a heavy bicycle. -> A bicycle traversed with the stated multiplicity and a weighted length bound.
- **step-pruned-old-graph** [claim](#stmt-prop-pruning) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-pruning): Summable expected deletion fractions and positive-probability selection, followed by local scale comparison. -> One uniformly small-loss old graph with expansion, girth, and short nonlaws.
- **step-supported-spreading** [claim](#stmt-lem-supported-spreading) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-supported-spreading): Diophantine torus separation gives orthogonal translates and supported isometry identities. -> Small-trace carriers and exact supported product identities.
- **step-fourier-envelope** [claim](#stmt-lem-fourier-envelope) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-fourier-envelope): Central Fourier derivative estimates, discrete summation by parts, and rotation packing. -> Uniform Fourier mass and finite frequency truncations.
- **step-product-sampler** [claim](#stmt-lem-product-sampler) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-product-sampler): Dominate coefficient mass by diffuse horizontal product marginals and put the residual in bounded matrix weights. -> Finite voltage-weight laws with independent horizontal coordinates.
- **step-polynomial-avoidance** [claim](#stmt-lem-polynomial-avoidance) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-polynomial-avoidance): Apply the maximal-atom polynomial zero bound to Heisenberg word coordinates. -> A small identity-holonomy probability including zero-weight edges.
- **step-regular-norm-box** [claim](#stmt-lem-heisenberg-folner-test) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-heisenberg-folner-test): Boundary-strip counting and averages of translated finite compressions. -> Finite-box tests approximating the full regular operator norm.
- **step-support-rank** [claim](#stmt-lem-moment-support-rank) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-moment-support-rank): Map a separated-net graph onto first homology using short null replacements. -> A support cycle-rank bound.
- **step-repeated-walk-count** [claim](#stmt-lem-repeated-edge-count) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-repeated-edge-count): Explore the rooted core and encode core steps, tree descents, and forced returns. -> A high-moment walk-count bound.
- **step-centered-concentration** [claim](#stmt-lem-centered-voltage-block) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-centered-voltage-block): Finite compression, singleton cancellation, support-rank control, and trace Markov bounds. -> A summable heightwise full-norm failure probability.
- **step-sampled-symbols** [claim](#stmt-prop-sampled-symbols) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-centered-voltage-block-2): Common-denominator rescaling, simultaneous failure bounds, and diagonal stabilization of finite edge choices. -> Simultaneous spectral approximation and voltage girth with a sharp refined alphabet.
- **step-private-completion** [claim](#stmt-lem-private-completion) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-private-completion): Join components by private subdivided paths and add loops generating the deck group. -> A connected voltage cover preserving local girth and recovery.
- **step-word-diagrams** [claim](#stmt-lem-word-diagram) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-word-diagram): Build a punctured-sphere word map, pair inverse occurrences, and take a planar dual. -> A literal exterior-word diagram with controlled filling cost and its converse.
- **step-null-path-closure** [claim](#stmt-lem-null-path-closes) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-null-path-closes): Minimize filling cost, remove coincident projected occurrences and bridges, then apply exceptional-vertex curvature. -> Null-labeled source paths have equal endpoints.
- **step-graphical-realization** [claim](#stmt-prop-graphical-realization) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-graphical-realization): Map path labels to group elements and use closure and private-letter rigidity. -> Embedded cover and deck subgroup with identified sheet stabilizers.
- **step-sheet-polygons** [claim](#stmt-lem-sheet-polygon) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-sheet-polygon): Reduce diagrams without moving contact endpoints and count compressed exterior arcs. -> Fixed-contact polygon bounds including private endpoints.
- **step-allowed-removed-sets** [claim](#stmt-lem-allowed-domain-sets) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-allowed-domain-sets): Shortest-path trees, local girth exclusion, and degree growth bounds. -> Forest incidence neighborhoods and removed-ball cardinality bounds.
- **step-finite-peeling** [claim](#stmt-lem-sheet-peeling) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-sheet-peeling): Assume every sheet has a distant contact and construct a polygon violating the exterior budget. -> A finite peeling order with overlap in allowed removed sets.
- **step-cycle-coordinates** [claim](#stmt-lem-sheet-cycle-coordinates) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-sheet-cycle-coordinates): Decompose finite cycles by relators and use peeled forest support to prove injectivity. -> Unique finite-support sheet cycle coordinates over commutative rings.
- **step-simultaneous-domains** [claim](#stmt-prop-domains) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-domains): Finite satisfiability from peeling and a diagonal limit of membership coordinates. -> Disjoint retained domains and a residual Cayley forest.
- **step-incidence-coordinates** [claim](#stmt-lem-sheet-incidences) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-sheet-incidences): Solve the sheet coset from a physical vertex and a specified base type. -> Sheet-incidence bijection and old-layer multiplicity bound.
- **step-exact-cone** [claim](#stmt-lem-cone-exact) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-cone-exact): Separate spoke boundaries and apply finite cycle coordinates. -> Exact augmented cellular chains over commutative rings.
- **step-resolution-comparison** [claim](#stmt-lem-free-resolution-comparison) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-free-resolution-comparison): Lift maps and differences basiswise through exact free complexes. -> Comparison chain maps and chain homotopies preserved by tensor and Hom.
- **step-torsion-obstruction** [claim](#stmt-lem-no-prime-torsion) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-no-prime-torsion): Compare a hypothetical short free resolution with the prime cyclic periodic resolution. -> Torsion-freeness of the graphical group.
- **step-explicit-detector** [claim](#stmt-lem-explicit-heisenberg-detector) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-explicit-heisenberg-detector): Verify the cocycle identity and evaluate on the oriented bar torus cycle. -> A primitive integral detector for the ordered torus.
- **step-restriction-surjection** [claim](#stmt-lem-degree-two-restriction) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-degree-two-restriction): Construct a free cone resolution and identify the resulting cochain map with subgroup restriction. -> Surjectivity of integral cohomology restriction in the required degree.
- **step-integral-detector** [claim](#stmt-prop-torsion-detector) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-torsion-detector): Extend the Heisenberg detector and retain its torus evaluation through the deck embedding. -> A torsion-free graphical group with an embedded detected torus.
- **step-trimming** [claim](#stmt-lem-assembly-trimming) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-assembly-trimming): Bound finite-coordinate projection loss by trace and estimate the compression defect. -> Uniform trimming tending to zero and almost multiplicative compression.
- **step-synthesis** [claim](#stmt-lem-assembly-synthesis) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-assembly-synthesis): Pointwise Cauchy–Schwarz with bounded indexed multiplicity, followed by heightwise norm summation. -> Bounded active-carrier synthesis and a small physical transport error.
- **step-incidence-repair** [claim](#stmt-lem-deleted-incidence) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-deleted-incidence): Replace the constant-coordinate block and compress the centered block orthogonally. -> Nearby contractions preserving normalized constants and the centered gap.
- **step-profile-realization** [claim](#stmt-lem-finite-profile-realization) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-finite-profile-realization): Use finite constant-extraction words and Cayley polynomials, controlling sampling, forest, deletion, and product errors. -> Finite reduced group-algebra approximants for the required band profiles.
- **step-source-membership** [claim](#stmt-lem-source-reduced-membership) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-source-reduced-membership): Realize finite meshes at successive accuracies and use uniform continuity for all path values. -> The source algebra maps into the reduced norm quotient.
- **step-supported-transport** [claim](#stmt-lem-supported-bott-rotation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-supported-bott-rotation): Multiply an explicit stabilized supported projection path. -> Equality of the original and transported Bott differences.
- **step-coordinate-equality** [claim](#stmt-lem-eventual-coordinate-equality) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-eventual-coordinate-equality): Lift a finite stabilized quotient witness, cut spectra, and polar-normalize corners. -> Exact equality in every sufficiently late coordinate algebra.
- **step-reduced-bott-vanishing** [claim](#stmt-prop-reduced-bott-vanishing) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-reduced-bott-vanishing): Combine finite parity cancellation with supported physical transport and eventual coordinate equality. -> Integral torus Bott vanishing in sufficiently late graphical group algebras.
- **step-finite-subgroup** [claim](#stmt-lem-finite-subgroup-witness) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-finite-subgroup-witness): Adjoin finite polynomial supports and repair the witness by functional calculus inside the subgroup algebra. -> The exact equality already holds in a finitely generated subgroup.
- **step-main-counterexample** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-finite-subgroup-witness-2): Retain a finite Bott witness, restrict and orient the detector, then apply the torus index pairing and subgroup assembly naturality. -> An infinite-order geometric class with integral zero reduced assembly image.

          "anchor": "stmt-prop-tower-labels",
          "exact_quote": "For every fixed finite type set and every sufficiently large $s$ as above, the construction has the following properties.*\n\n1.  *The auxiliary state sizes are <a href=\"#eq:state-sizes\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:state-sizes\">[eq:state-sizes]</a>. The total degrees are at most $T2^b d$ on A and $T2^bD$ on B.*\n\n\n2.  *The labeling is folded: no two outgoing oriented edges at a vertex have the same label. Its signed alphabet satisfies $$\\label{eq:sharp-alphabet}\n     |\\mathcal S|\\le 2TM2^b d2^r,\n     \\qquad\n     \\frac{\\sqrt{|\\mathcal S|}}q\n     \\le 2^{-.000025s+O(\\log s)+O_{T,b}(1)}\\longrightarrow0.$$*\n\n3.  *At every height the fraction of removed vertices on each side is at most $\\varepsilon_{\\rm gate}+\\varepsilon_{\\rm sep}$. This bound can be made arbitrarily small, uniformly over heights and all table choices. The separate-parity and terminal gate conclusions of Lemma <a href=\"#lem:gate-deletions\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:gate-deletions\">4</a> hold.*\n\n4.  *Every immersed path in $X^{\\rm good}$ starting at height $i$ and having length at least $\\lceil1.002m_i\\rceil$ is the unique immersed path with that oriented word anywhere in $X^{\\rm good}$. This is a global assertion over all heights and components.",

        }

          "exact_quote": "Let $W$ be a nonempty closed edge path in $X$, reduced as a based edge path: no two consecutive traversals in its linear ordering are mutually inverse.           "anchor": "stmt-lem-supported-spreading",
          "exact_quote": "For sufficiently large initial height, the family of projections $$\\lambda(y)^jE_i\\lambda(y)^{-j},\\qquad \\left\\lvert j\\right\\rvert\\leq J_i,$$ is pairwise orthogonal. Consequently $$E_iT_i^*T_iE_i=E_i,\\qquad P_i=T_iE_iT_i^*$$ is a projection, and $$\\label{eq:spread-trace}\n \\tau_{\\mathsf H}(P_i)=\\tau_{\\mathsf H}(E_i)\n \\leq C_R2^{-700H_i}.$$ If $f$ is supported in both enlarged rectangles at heights $i$ and $k$, and $g$ in both enlarged rectangles at heights $k$ and $j$, then $$(T_i fT_k^*)(T_k gT_j^*)=T_i(fg)T_j^*,\\qquad\n (T_i fT_k^*)^*=T_k f^*T_i^*,\\qquad\n \\left\\lVert T_i fT_k^*\\right\\rVert\\leq\\left\\lVert f\\right\\rVert.$$",

        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-fourier-envelope",
          "exact_quote": "Let $\\left\\lvert i-k\\right\\rvert\\leq1$, and let $f$ belong to the profile family <a href=\"#eq:profile-family\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:profile-family\">[eq:profile-family]</a>, expressed at reference height $i$. Write $$T_i fT_k^*=\\sum_{n,l,c}A_{n,l,c}\\lambda(x^ny^lz^c),\n \\qquad N=N_i,\\quad Z=Z_i,\\quad J=J_i.$$ For every pair of nonnegative integers $A,P$, with $P\\geq3$, $$\\label{eq:weighted-central-envelope}\n \\sum_c(1+\\left\\lvert c\\right\\rvert/Z)^A\\left\\lVert A_{n,l,c}\\right\\rVert\n \\leq C_{A,P}N^{-1}(1+\\left\\lvert n\\right\\rvert/N)^{-P}\n       (1+J\\left\\lVert n\\alpha\\right\\rVert_{\\mathbb R/\\mathbb Z})^{-3},\n \\qquad \\left\\lvert l\\right\\rvert\\leq C_bJ,$$ and the coefficients vanish for larger $\\left\\lvert l\\right\\rvert$. The constants are uniform in height and in the compact profile family. In particular these symbols have a uniformly bounded Fourier one-norm. For each $\\eta>0$ they can be truncated, with one-norm error at most $\\eta$, to $$\\label{eq:fourier-box}\n \\left\\lvert n\\right\\rvert\\leq L_\\eta N,\\qquad \\left\\lvert l\\right\\rvert\\leq C_bJ,\\qquad\n \\left\\lvert c\\right\\rvert\\leq L_\\eta Z,$$ where $L_\\eta$ is independent of height.",
          "source_id": "PM-285.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-fourier-envelope",

          "anchor": "stmt-lem-product-sampler",
          "exact_quote": "For every fixed $\\eta>0$, each symbol in Lemma <a href=\"#lem:fourier-envelope\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:fourier-envelope\">30</a> has a finite distribution of pairs $(W,v)$, with $W\\in M_\\ell(\\mathbb C)$ and $v=x^ny^lz^c\\in\\mathsf H$, satisfying $$\\left\\lVert W\\right\\rVert\\leq M_\\eta,\\qquad\n \\left\\lVert\\mathbb E[W\\lambda(v)]-T_ifT_k^*\\right\\rVert_1\\leq\\eta.$$ The horizontal coordinates n and l are independent. Their maximal atoms satisfy $$\\label{eq:horizontal-atoms}\n \\sup_n\\Pr(n)=O_b(J_i/N_i),\\qquad\n \\sup_l\\Pr(l)=O_b(J_i^{-1}).$$ All possible group coordinates, including those of inverses, are bounded by $2^{a_0H_i}$ for a fixed exponent $a_0$, for example $a_0=502$, after increasing the initial height. The weights can additionally be restricted to one finite adjoint-closed net, at the expense of any prescribed further one-norm error. All constants and the net are independent of height.",

          "anchor": "stmt-lem-heisenberg-folner-test",
          "exact_quote": "Suppose a finite set of group elements and their inverses have coordinates bounded by $2^{a_0H}$, with $H\\geq1$. For every fixed B there is a finite box $\\mathcal B_H\\subset\\mathsf H$, of cardinality at most $2^{K_0H}$, such that $$\\frac{\\left\\lvert\\mathcal B_H\\triangle v\\mathcal B_H\\right\\rvert}\n      {\\left\\lvert\\mathcal B_H\\right\\rvert}\\leq C2^{-BH}$$ for all these elements. The exponent $K_0$ depends on $a_0,B$, but not on the number of elements.*\n\n\n*Let $A$ be a selfadjoint finite matrix of regular convolution sums whose convolution support is contained in the finite set just specified, with any fixed matrix amplification. Let R bound its absolute coefficient row sums; by selfadjointness it also bounds the column sums. If $Q_H$ retains the deck coordinates in $\\mathcal B_H$, then $$\\label{eq:full-regular-norm-test}\n \\left\\lVert Q_HAQ_H\\right\\rVert\\leq\\left\\lVert A\\right\\rVert\n \\leq\\left\\lVert Q_HAQ_H\\right\\rVert+C2^{-BH}R.$$ The same statement holds for rectangular matrices after selfadjoint off-diagonal doubling, using the larger row or column coefficient bound.",
          "source_id": "PM-285.1"
        }

          "anchor": "stmt-prop-sampled-symbols",
          "exact_quote": "Fix b, a finite matrix dimension, a positive accuracy $\\eta$, and a finite list of compact smooth symbol profiles and height selections as above. The list may include cutoffs, both parities, the initial-height selection, and any finite mesh of the adjacent rescaling and rotation families. Allocate a prescribed incidence type for every required same or adjacent layer symbol. For sufficiently large s and then sufficiently large initial height, the surviving base graph admits a voltage on every edge and finite-net matrix weights such that the following hold simultaneously.*\n\n1.  *The voltage cover has no nontrivial cyclically reduced closed walk of length at most $6.03m_i$ based at an old vertex of height i.*\n\n2.  *All weighted blocks may use the common denominator q. If $\\mathcal V_{\\tau,ik}$ is that normalized weighted convolution block and the prescribed symbol is $S_{\\tau,ik}=T_if_{\\tau,ik}T_k^*$, then $$\\left\\lVert\\mathcal V_{\\tau,ik}\n               -U_{\\tau,ik}\\otimes S_{\\tau,ik}\\right\\rVert\\leq\\eta$$ in the full regular operator norm, at every height. The tensor notation here means that each scalar incidence entry multiplies the same matrix-valued deck convolution symbol.*\n\n3.  *Weights are read from one finite set, independent of height. Appending their net indices to the existing edge labels preserves the labelled-graph uniqueness properties and gives $$\\log_2\\left\\lvert\\mathcal A\\right\\rvert\n     \\leq1.00005s+O(\\log s)+O_{b,\\eta,\\mathrm{profiles}}(1)\n     <\\log_2(q^2)$$ for sufficiently large s. In particular $\\sqrt{\\left\\lvert\\mathcal A\\right\\rvert}/q$ tends to zero as s increases after the fixed data. Zero-weight edges are retained in the voltage cover.",

          "anchor": "stmt-lem-private-completion",
          "exact_quote": "There is a connected, countable, folded labeled graph $X$ containing $X_{\\mathrm o}$ as its old subgraph, and an extension of the voltages, with the following properties.*\n\n1.  *Each added edge has its own new positive letter, used on no other base edge. Every new vertex has degree two and has only private incident edges. The maximum degree is at most $K_{b,\\epsilon}D+6$.*\n\n2.  *The function $m$ extends to the new vertices, remains at least $m_{\\min}$, and satisfies $|m(v)-m(w)|\\le a$ on every edge.*\n\n\n3.  *The voltage cover $\\widetilde X$, with edges $$\\label{eq:voltage-convention}\n     (v,h)\\longrightarrow(w,hh_e)$$ over $e\\colon v\\to w$, is connected. Its deck action is the left action $f(v,h)=(v,fh)$ of $\\mathsf H$.*\n\n4.  *Every simple cycle $C$ of $\\widetilde X$ satisfies $|C|>6.03m(v)$ at every vertex $v$ of $C$.*\n\n5.  *If two paths in $\\widetilde X$ have the same word and their projected occurrences in $X$ differ, neither contains a private edge. If they are immersed and start at $v,w$, their common length is less than both $Lm(v)$ and $Lm(w)$.*\n\n*The scale on the cover is the pullback of the scale on $X$.",

        }

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **invariant-detected-assembly-kernel**: The constructed finitely generated torsion-free group has a torus Dirac class of infinite order whose reduced analytic assembly image vanishes.
- **invariant-padded-prefix-recovery**: Matching oriented words on immersed surviving paths determine matching padded state prefixes at the stated interior radii, including comparisons across different heights.
- **invariant-sequential-expansion-control**: The selected table law gives every auxiliary incidence a uniform centered singular norm bound and controls the probability inflation of joint tests of current tables conditional on earlier updates.
- **invariant-domains-and-residual-forest**: Allowed removed sets can be chosen simultaneously so that retained sheet domains are pairwise disjoint and the remaining formal Cayley edges form a forest.
- **invariant-eventual-bott-vanishing**: Along the chosen compatible accuracy sequence, the embedded torus Bott generator vanishes integrally in every sufficiently late reduced group algebra.
- **invariant-finite-subgroup-retention**: An existing equality of finite projection classes over the ambient reduced group algebra can be retained in a finitely generated subgroup containing the original finitely generated subgroup.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-maximal-and-classical-novikov**: Treat the reduced assembly kernel as a counterexample to maximal rational injectivity or the classical Novikov conjecture. -> The same detected class survives rationally under maximal assembly; the reduced quotient kills its maximal image.
- **anti-finite-presentation-and-exactness**: Use the constructed group as a finitely presented exact counterexample. -> The conclusion supplies finite generation without establishing finite presentation or exactness.
- **anti-scalar-projection-inference**: Infer a nontrivial scalar projection from the vanishing of the matrix Bott class. -> Scalar projections concern a different obstruction, and a scalar projection alone does not identify an assembly kernel class.
- **anti-arbitrary-current-answer-conditioning**: Apply the sequential probability inflation bound after conditioning on arbitrary answers already obtained from the same current block. -> The estimate controls joint tests of current variables relative to earlier updates, rather than arbitrary adaptive conditioning within that block.
- **anti-truncated-coefficient-inversion**: Invert a truncated coefficient difference when checking terminal compatibility. -> Full-field invertibility does not supply the asserted invertibility after truncation.
- **anti-borel-carrier-membership**: Treat auxiliary Borel carrier projections as elements of the reduced group algebra. -> The carrier projections support Hilbert-space estimates, while reduced-algebra membership requires continuous functions and finite formulas.
- **anti-formal-infinite-class-sum**: Conclude Bott vanishing by subtracting formal infinite sums of classes without constructing bounded operator families. -> Formal class manipulations omit the required operator boundedness, quotient membership, and finite-additivity justification.
- **anti-finite-experiments-as-global-certificate**: Use agreement on finite tower truncations as a certificate of the complete assembly counterexample. -> Finite truncations do not certify simultaneous choices over the infinite tower, exact quotient membership, or the existence of the final analytic witness.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `not_applicable`. The selected result is the full existential assembly counterexample, whose construction uses an infinite tower, simultaneous choices by diagonal limits, and exact equality in a norm sequence quotient before extracting a finite subgroup witness. A finite local identity check would not certify that result. The simultaneous-selection proof states: “The diagonal limit satisfies every condition, because each condition depends on finitely many edge choices.” The reading conventions further state: “Matrix dimensions remain fixed along each approximation sequence in which quotient $K$-theory is used; finite generation is extracted only after an exact finite witness has been obtained.” These existence and limiting steps do not provide a finite constructive input-output contract for the complete theorem.

<a id="pm-s5"></a>
## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | The explicit Heisenberg coordinate law supports an exact arithmetic routine for multiplying group elements and evaluating finite voltage paths. This routine implements a local component of the construction. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The voltage cover supplies graph geometry, but its countable construction gives no game dynamics, collision model, or finite simulation specification. |
| tools | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | The finite-support circuit decomposition gives a concrete graph-analysis procedure for expressing a supplied cycle chain as a sum of circuit chains over a commutative coefficient ring. |
| algorithms | True | strong | adaptable | inferred | Distinct affine evaluations over a finite field determine the input and evaluation coordinates uniquely. The paper extends this local operation to causal block reconstruction with known table keys and correction offsets. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The direct sum of sheet cycle modules specifies finite-support mathematical coordinates, but the paper supplies no representation or access procedure for enumerating the ambient sheets and their cycle coordinates. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The regular representations act on Hilbert spaces, and finite Heisenberg boxes serve norm estimates. No hardware layout, arithmetic circuit, or accelerator execution contract is constructed. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The coefficient pools enforce distinctness and bounded functional multiplicity for recovery and expansion. They provide no secrecy, adversarial hardness, or cryptographic security construction. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The sampled matrix weights approximate prescribed compact symbols in the full regular operator norm. Their construction contains no training objective, learned predictor, or generalization guarantee. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The sequential selection law proves existence of expanding tables with controlled probability inflation. It does not specify an optimization objective or an effective search procedure for a globally admissible tower. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The simultaneous disjoint domains arise through finite satisfiability and a diagonal limit. No communication protocol, distributed ownership procedure, or fault model accompanies those choices. |
| other | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The main application is an existence counterexample in operator algebraic topology. Its infinite tower and quotient argument do not provide a finite engineering implementation of the resulting group and assembly kernel class. |

If $R_Q$ contains an old vertex of layer $i$, then $\\left\\lvert R_Q\\right\\rvert\\leq2^{10H_i}$. A private singleton removes no old coordinate.*\n\n*No equivariance of the choices $R_Q$ is asserted or needed.",

## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-existential-group-scope** (counterexample_scope): The counterexample concerns a constructed finitely generated torsion-free discrete group and its detected torus class; it does not assert failure for every torsion-free group. Blocked use: ['Rejecting assembly injectivity for an arbitrary torsion-free input group.']
- **limit-completion-and-novikov-scope** (formal_scope): Reduced assembly vanishing does not disprove maximal rational injectivity or the classical Novikov conjecture. Blocked use: ['Transferring the reduced kernel conclusion to maximal assembly.', 'Claiming failure of oriented homotopy invariance of higher signatures.']
- **limit-finite-presentation-and-exactness** (unknown): Finite presentation and exactness of the constructed group are ABSENT from the established conclusions. Blocked use: ['Using the construction as a finitely presented exact counterexample.']
- **limit-scalar-projection-inference** (formal_scope): The proof uses matrix-valued Bott representatives and a separate integral detector. Its kernel argument does not establish a nontrivial scalar projection. Blocked use: ['Inferring scalar projection existence from the matrix Bott calculation.', 'Replacing the detector by a scalar projection obstruction to surjectivity.']
- **limit-finite-field-recovery-preconditions** (hypothesis): Local recovery requires distinct coefficients in the full finite field and known evaluation data. Blocked use: ['Dividing by a zero coefficient difference.', 'Applying the recovery formula to arbitrary coefficients outside the specified field model.']
- **limit-universal-rules-after-gate-deletion** (hypothesis): The universal padded equations and the surviving-slot gate guarantees apply after the specified gate deletions. Blocked use: ['Using universal-equation recovery on an auxiliary edge whose forced choices were necessary.', 'Omitting gate checks while retaining the surviving-slot conclusions.']
- **limit-recovery-is-prefix-and-radius-dependent** (hypothesis): Common oriented words recover padded prefixes at the stated interior radii on immersed paths in the gate-surviving graph. Blocked use: ['Recovering a complete endpoint state from an arbitrarily short common word.', 'Applying the recovery conclusion to paths with immediate reversals or outside the surviving graph.']
- **limit-global-uniqueness-needs-height-separation** (hypothesis): Global uniqueness of sufficiently long words is a conclusion for the graph after both gate and separation deletions. Blocked use: ['Treating padded-prefix agreement alone as identification of vertex height.', 'Extending the uniqueness conclusion to short words or to the undeleted auxiliary graph.']
- **limit-current-table-conditioning** (hypothesis): The probability inflation estimate concerns joint tests of current tables conditional on earlier updates; it does not allow arbitrary conditioning on answers from the same current block. Blocked use: ['Applying the inflation bound to a next query after arbitrary current-block answers have been prescribed.']
- **limit-pruning-predictability** (hypothesis): Reconstruction must determine current keys and arguments before exposing the current tables. Later minimality and consistency requirements remain final predicates rather than extra conditioning of the current law. Blocked use: ['Using current reconstructed outputs to select current tests.', 'Conditioning the sampling law on eventual minimality before applying the rank estimate.']
- **limit-terminal-prefix-independence** (hypothesis): Terminal compatibility estimates require distinct tested keys, previously known input arguments, open terminal gates, and the actual active random prefix. Blocked use: ['Multiplying compatibility probabilities for repeated table variables.', 'Counting deterministic suffix bits as fresh random constraints.', 'Inverting truncated coefficient differences.']
- **limit-global-pruning-selection** (nonconstructive): Small losses in every layer are obtained by summable expectation bounds and positive-probability selection, rather than by an explicitly supplied universal table assignment. Blocked use: ['Claiming that local reconstruction specifies the entire pruned expanding tower.', 'Treating a finite sample of layers as a certificate of simultaneous pruning at all heights.']
- **limit-heisenberg-law-quantification** (hypothesis): The bicycle conclusion assumes a nonempty closed path reduced as a based path and lawhood under every independent Heisenberg edge assignment. Blocked use: ['Inferring a heavy bicycle merely because one voltage assignment has trivial holonomy.', 'Dropping based reduction or the nonempty-path assumption.']
- **limit-sampling-variable-reuse** (hypothesis): Different unoriented edges are sampled independently, while repeated traversals reuse the same voltage and reverse traversals use its inverse. Zero matrix weights do not remove the voltage variable. Blocked use: ['Resampling a voltage on each traversal.', 'Discarding zero-weight edges from holonomy tests.', 'Assuming that matrix weights and voltages are independent.']
- **limit-full-regular-norm-certificate** (approximation): Finite Heisenberg compression estimates the full regular norm only with the stated convolution-support bounds, coefficient bounds, and boundary-error term. Blocked use: ['Equating an arbitrary finite compression norm with the infinite regular norm.', 'Ignoring the coefficient row and column loss in the compression error.']
- **limit-concentration-before-girth-conditioning** (hypothesis): Centered block concentration is proved under independent sampling without conditioning on voltage girth; simultaneous selection uses the separate failure bounds afterward. Blocked use: ['Reusing the centered moment bound under an unproved law conditioned on voltage-girth success.']
- **limit-infinite-voltage-selection** (nonconstructive): The simultaneous voltage and weight assignment over all heights is obtained by a diagonal limit of finite satisfying assignments. Blocked use: ['Presenting finite edge tests as an effective generator of the complete voltage assignment.']
- **limit-formal-cayley-cells** (formal_scope): The geometric and cycle arguments retain formal oriented Cayley edges as distinct cells even when their unordered endpoints coincide. Blocked use: ['Replacing the formal Cayley graph by an undirected simple graph before applying diagram, forest, or cycle-coordinate arguments.']
- **limit-domains-compactness-and-equivariance** (nonconstructive): Simultaneous disjoint domains are selected by finite satisfiability and a diagonal limit. Equivariance of the removed sets is not asserted. Blocked use: ['Treating the domain choice as an equivariant partition algorithm.', 'Computing global domain membership from a finite peeling order alone.']
- **limit-finite-support-cone-chains** (formal_scope): Cycle coordinates and the exact cone-chain complex use finite-support chains over commutative coefficient rings; cone exactness does not require the compactness choice of domains. Blocked use: ['Replacing the direct sum of cycle modules by unrestricted infinite chain sums.', 'Claiming exactness for an unspecified completed chain complex.', 'Inferring contractibility solely from cellular-chain exactness.']
- **limit-borel-carriers-and-synthesis** (formal_scope): Borel carriers are auxiliary support operators. Reduced-algebra formulas use continuous functions and finite sums, and bounded synthesis is established on active carriers. Blocked use: ['Assuming Borel carriers lie in the reduced group algebra.', 'Assuming unrestricted untrimmed sheet summation is bounded or isometric.']
- **limit-finite-profile-realization** (approximation): Finite formulas realize a prescribed finite collection of supported profiles with a fixed matrix size and a target error; reduced quotient membership additionally uses uniform path continuity. Blocked use: ['Claiming that arbitrary bounded model operators belong to the reduced quotient.', 'Replacing uniform approximation of the chosen homotopies by unrelated pointwise approximations.']
- **limit-parity-cancellation-not-formal-infinite-sums** (formal_scope): Parity cancellation uses actual bounded operator families in the specified source algebra, reduced quotient membership, and finite additivity of classes. Blocked use: ['Subtracting formal infinite sums of classes without constructing the operator families and homotopies.']
- **limit-supported-transport-assumptions** (hypothesis): Supported Bott transport requires a projection difference and a bounded transport acting as a scalar on the matrix factor and preserving that difference through its initial product. Blocked use: ['Applying the rotation identity to arbitrary matrix-valued transports.', 'Omitting the supported-isometry relation because the transport is bounded.']
- **limit-fixed-size-eventual-coordinate-equality** (hypothesis): Eventual coordinate equality assumes unital coordinate algebras and projections in a fixed matrix size. It yields equality only sufficiently far along the sequence. Blocked use: ['Concluding equality at every coordinate.', 'Allowing matrix sizes to grow without an additional argument.', 'Claiming an explicit coordinate threshold from the existential quotient witness.']
- **limit-finite-subgroup-after-exact-witness** (nonconstructive): Finite subgroup retention starts with fixed finite projections over a finitely generated subgroup and an already established ambient equality. It retains a finite witness rather than computing arbitrary group-algebra classes. Blocked use: ['Extracting the final subgroup from approximate Bott cancellation before an exact equality witness exists.', 'Treating subgroup retention as a general decision procedure for equality in operator K-theory.']

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We disprove rational injectivity of the coefficient-free reduced Baum–Connes assembly map for torsion-free groups. Specifically, we construct a finitely generated torsion-free discrete group whose degree-zero assembly map has a kernel class of infinite order.

<a id="sec:introduction"></a>
### Introduction

The Baum–Connes assembly map relates the topology of a discrete group to the operator algebra generated by its regular representation. For a torsion-free discrete group $G_{\mathrm{inj}}$, its coefficient-free form is $$\mu_{G_{\mathrm{inj}}}^r\colon K_*(BG_{\mathrm{inj}})\longrightarrow K_*(C_r^*(G_{\mathrm{inj}})).$$ Here $BG_{\mathrm{inj}}$ is a classifying space, $K_*(BG_{\mathrm{inj}})$ is complex topological $K$-homology with compact support, namely the directed limit over finite subcomplexes, and $C_r^*(G_{\mathrm{inj}})$ is the reduced group $C^*$-algebra. The Baum–Connes conjecture asserts that this map is an isomorphism (P. Baum, Connes, and Higson 1994) <a id="pm-cite-1"></a>[[BCH94](#ref-BCH94)]. In this formulation the coefficient algebra is $\mathbb C$ with its trivial action; the more general conjecture with coefficients allows a $G_{\mathrm{inj}}$-algebra $A$ and has target $K_*(A\rtimes_r G_{\mathrm{inj}})$.

<a id="stmt-thm-main"></a>
<div id="thm:main" class="theorem">

**Theorem 1**. *There exist a finitely generated torsion-free discrete group $G_{\mathrm{inj}}$, an injective homomorphism $i:\mathbb Z^2\to G_{\mathrm{inj}}$, and a class $c\in H^2(BG_{\mathrm{inj}};\mathbb Z)$ such that $$\left\langle(Bi)^*c,[T^2]\right\rangle=1,\qquad
i_*(\beta)=0\quad\text{in }K_0(C_r^*G_{\mathrm{inj}}),$$ where $\beta$ is a rank-zero Bott generator of $K_0(C_r^*\mathbb Z^2)$. In particular, $h=(Bi)_*[D_{T^2}]$ has infinite order in $K_0(BG_{\mathrm{inj}})$, while its reduced analytic assembly image is zero. Here $[D_{T^2}]$ is the spin Dirac $K$-homology class of the oriented torus.*

</div>

The theorem gives an integral reduced assembly kernel class of infinite order, and hence disproves rational injectivity of coefficient-free reduced assembly. Its two assertions have different roles. The cohomology class extends the orientation class of the embedded torus and detects its Dirac class by an index pairing. The vanishing of the corresponding rank-zero Bott class is proved by a separate operator-algebra construction. The group is finitely generated; finite presentation is not part of the conclusion.

<a id="pm-h-3"></a>
#### The distinction from earlier counterexamples

Baum and Connes introduced this index-theoretic comparison in a preprint circulated in 1982 and published in 2000 (P. Baum and Connes 2000, sec. 1 and editors’ note) <a id="pm-cite-2"></a>[[BC00](#ref-BC00)]. The formulation using the classifying space for proper actions was developed with Higson (P. Baum, Connes, and Higson 1994, Conjecture 3.15) <a id="pm-cite-3"></a>[[BCH94](#ref-BCH94)]. In the torsion-free case that space gives the assembly map displayed above.

The conjecture is known for substantial classes of groups. Higson and Kasparov prove it with separable coefficients for groups acting properly by affine isometries on Hilbert space, including countable amenable groups (Higson and Kasparov 2001, Theorem 9.1 and Corollary 9.2) <a id="pm-cite-4"></a>[[HK01](#ref-HK01)]. The coefficient-free hyperbolic case was established by Mineyev–Yu using Lafforgue’s Banach-$KK$ methods (Mineyev and Yu 2002, Theorem 20) <a id="pm-cite-5"></a>[[MY02](#ref-MY02)]; Lafforgue subsequently proved the version with coefficients (Lafforgue 2012, Theorem 0.4) <a id="pm-cite-6"></a>[[Laf12](#ref-Laf12)]. Guentner, Higson, and Weinberger prove split injectivity with coefficients for countable linear groups (Guentner, Higson, and Weinberger 2005, Theorem 3) <a id="pm-cite-7"></a>[[GHW05](#ref-GHW05)]. These results describe major settings where the kernel constructed here cannot occur.

Higson, Lafforgue, and Skandalis constructed counterexamples in several assembly settings (Higson, Lafforgue, and Skandalis 2002) <a id="pm-cite-8"></a>[[HLS02](#ref-HLS02)]. For discrete groups, their examples concern failure of surjectivity with nontrivial commutative coefficients; their paper explicitly distinguishes this from the conjecture for groups alone. Its groupoid examples also have failures of injectivity, but the groupoid and coefficient-free group assertions have different domains and targets. Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a> concerns injectivity with coefficient algebra $\mathbb C$.

Gromov’s graph-labeled small-cancellation strategy connects expander geometry to group constructions (Gromov 2003, sec. 4.8) <a id="pm-cite-9"></a>[[Gro03](#ref-Gro03)]. Graphical small-cancellation methods subsequently gave further group constructions with coefficient obstructions. For example, Osajda’s graphical constructions, together with the coefficient result of Willett and Yu, give a suitable coefficient algebra for which reduced assembly is injective and not surjective (Osajda 2020, Corollary 3.4) <a id="pm-cite-10"></a>[[Osa20](#ref-Osa20)] <a id="pm-cite-11"></a>[[WY12](#ref-WY12)](Willett and Yu 2012, Corollary 1.7). This provides relevant geometric precedent without supplying the coefficient-free kernel required here.

The exact crossed-product reformulation of Baum, Guentner, and Willett was designed to address the failures involving coefficients (P. Baum, Guentner, and Willett 2016) <a id="pm-cite-12"></a>[[BGW16](#ref-BGW16)]. It replaces the reduced crossed product by the minimal exact Morita-compatible crossed product. That reformulation, the reduced coefficient-free conjecture, and the maximal assembly map must be kept distinct when interpreting the present construction.

<a id="pm-h-4"></a>
#### Maximal assembly and the Novikov conjecture

Write $\mu_{G_{\mathrm{inj}}}^{\max}$ for assembly with target $K_*(C^*_{\max}(G_{\mathrm{inj}}))$, and let $\lambda_{G_{\mathrm{inj}}}$ be the regular quotient from $C^*_{\max}(G_{\mathrm{inj}})$ to $C_r^*(G_{\mathrm{inj}})$. By the completion comparison (Land 2015, Remark 2.6) <a id="pm-cite-13"></a>[[Land15](#ref-Land15)], $$\mu_{G_{\mathrm{inj}}}^r=(\lambda_{G_{\mathrm{inj}}})_*\mu_{G_{\mathrm{inj}}}^{\max}.$$ Hanke and Schick prove maximal rational nonvanishing for every discrete group whenever a $K$-homology class is detected by the subring of rational cohomology generated in degrees at most two (Hanke and Schick 2008, Theorem 1.2) <a id="pm-cite-14"></a>[[HS08](#ref-HS08)]. Consequently the class $h$ in Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a> satisfies $$\mu_{G_{\mathrm{inj}}}^{\max}(h)\otimes1\ne0,
  \qquad
  (\lambda_{G_{\mathrm{inj}}})_*\bigl(\mu_{G_{\mathrm{inj}}}^{\max}(h)\bigr)=0.$$ The name *strong Novikov conjecture* is used for both maximal and reduced rational injectivity in the literature (P. Baum, Connes, and Higson 1994, sec. 7) <a id="pm-cite-15"></a>[[BCH94](#ref-BCH94)] <a id="pm-cite-16"></a>[[BGW16](#ref-BGW16)](P. Baum, Guentner, and Willett 2016, sec. 8.3, footnote 6). Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a> disproves the reduced formulation, while maximal assembly detects the same class $h$. The classical Novikov conjecture concerns oriented homotopy invariance of higher signatures. Our result disproves neither maximal rational injectivity nor this classical assertion; in particular, higher signatures associated to low-degree cohomology already have the homotopy invariance established in (Hanke and Schick 2008, Corollary 1.3) <a id="pm-cite-17"></a>[[HS08](#ref-HS08)].

Antonini, Buss, Engel, and Siebenand extend the low-degree nonvanishing result to suitable exotic group $C^*$-algebras (Antonini et al. 2021, Theorem B) <a id="pm-cite-18"></a>[[ABES21](#ref-ABES21)]. Their corrected statement assumes a *finitely presented* group and applies, in particular, to the group algebra of the minimal exact Morita-compatible crossed-product functor. For exact groups this algebra agrees with the reduced group algebra. The asserted agreement for all groups was affected by the gap recorded in the erratum of Buss, Echterhoff, and Willett (Buss, Echterhoff, and Willett 2021) <a id="pm-cite-19"></a>[[BEW21err](#ref-BEW21err)]. Accordingly we use the corrected version of (Antonini et al. 2021) <a id="pm-cite-20"></a>[[ABES21](#ref-ABES21)], arXiv:1905.07730v3, rather than its original reduced-algebra claim. Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a> asserts finite generation; it supplies neither finite presentation nor exactness.

A further distinction concerns projections. For a countable torsion-free group, a nontrivial projection in the scalar algebra $C_r^*(G_{\mathrm{inj}})$ would obstruct surjectivity of coefficient-free assembly (Kaad and Proietti 2022, Corollary 1.6) <a id="pm-cite-21"></a>[[KP22](#ref-KP22)]. Such a projection alone does not identify an assembly kernel class. Here matrix-valued Bott representatives are used to prove a specific vanishing while the integral detector proves that the corresponding geometric class survives.

<a id="pm-h-5"></a>
#### The construction

In the operator model, localized rank-zero Bott representatives occupy coordinates indexed by heights $i\geq0$. The analytic aim is to cancel the class at height zero by moving bounded families of these representatives. Rotations and rescalings on the disjoint adjacent pairs $(0,1),(2,3),\ldots$ move the even family to the odd family. The pairs $(1,2),(3,4),\ldots$ then move the odd family to the positive-even family. These two homotopies identify the even family with the family obtained by removing its height-zero term. Finite additivity therefore forces that term to vanish. The families must be actual bounded operators, their homotopies must belong to a reduced-algebra norm quotient, and the resulting equality must yield a finite witness in one group algebra. These requirements determine the construction below.

The proof connects three ingredients. Its graph-labeling stage is related to the finite-alphabet separation method of Osajda (Osajda 2020, sec. 2) <a id="pm-cite-22"></a>[[Osa20](#ref-Osa20)]; the layered graph, the sequential expansion constraints, and the simultaneous spectral requirements below are additional features. The voltage-cover language follows Gross and Gross–Tucker (Gross 1974; Gross and Tucker 1977) <a id="pm-cite-23"></a>[[Gross74](#ref-Gross74), [GT77](#ref-GT77)]. Neither reference supplies the analytic realization needed here.

First, a layered graph is labeled by interleaved finite streams. Two incident labels recover successive blocks of a vertex state. The recovery procedure gives uniqueness for sufficiently long immersed words, while the random tables can be selected with a uniform expansion gap. Products of these incidences and their adjoints will later isolate the normalized constant coordinates on which the Bott families act. Pruning removes short coherent returns and configurations that would obstruct the required overlap bounds. The central issue is to retain enough independent constraints after conditioning on expansion. Proposition <a href="#prop:tower-labels" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:tower-labels">7</a>, Proposition <a href="#prop:sequential-law" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:sequential-law">13</a>, and Proposition <a href="#prop:pruning" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:pruning">27</a> give the labeling, selection law, and counting estimates with compatible parameters.

Second, the graph is given voltages in the integral Heisenberg group $$\mathsf H=\langle x,y,z\mid [x,y]=z,\ [x,z]=[y,z]=1\rangle.$$ A voltage assigns a group element $h_e$ to an oriented base edge $v\to w$; its lift sends $(v,f)$ to $(w,fh_e)$, and the reverse edge uses $h_e^{-1}$. Short circuits surviving pruning are not laws in $\mathsf H$. The voltage choice simultaneously separates these circuits and approximates the required spectral averages. Continuous matrix-valued symbols spread the Bott class across spectral bands, and finite sampling is controlled in the full regular operator norm. Proposition <a href="#prop:sampled-symbols" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:sampled-symbols">29</a> supplies this analytic selection. The Heisenberg group provides a torsion-free source for the voltage cover and also contains the commuting pair $(z,x)$ used for the final torus.

Third, the geometric realization keeps the Heisenberg subgroup embedded and makes its degree-two detector extend to the ambient group. A *sheet* is a translate of the embedded cover in the Cayley graph. Integral cycle decompositions in a complex built from these sheets control both torsion and restriction in cohomology; see Proposition <a href="#prop:graphical-realization" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:graphical-realization">40</a> and Proposition <a href="#prop:torsion-detector" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:torsion-detector">52</a>. Disjoint domains in the sheets allow the spectral operators to act on the group’s regular Hilbert space. The number of excluded coordinates in each sheet is bounded, and the spectral support projections have traces small enough relative to these bounds that the loss on the supported operators is small in norm. The remaining Cayley edges form a forest, whose contribution is controlled by the ratio of the square root of the label count to the incidence normalizer. This is why the finite alphabet must retain a sharp margin throughout the graph construction. These estimates realize the bounded Bott families and their homotopies in the reduced-algebra norm quotient. Proposition <a href="#prop:reduced-bott-vanishing" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:reduced-bott-vanishing">60</a> then obtains integral Bott vanishing in an actual reduced group algebra. The finite-witness argument retains this equality in the group generated by the finitely many group elements in polynomial approximations to that witness, together with the Heisenberg generators. This finitely generated subgroup is the group in Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>.

Figure <a href="#fig:proof-dependencies" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="fig:proof-dependencies">1</a> records the main dependencies.

<figure id="fig:proof-dependencies">
<embed src="figures/proof-dependencies.pdf" />
<figcaption>Principal dependencies in the construction. The stream rules, sequential selection, and pruning precede the joint voltage and spectral choice. Geometric realization supports both the integral detector and the reduced Bott cancellation. A finite witness retains both properties in a finitely generated subgroup.</figcaption>
</figure>

- Down arrow to Node 2: "Uniform gap and pruning \n Sequential table selection; short returns removed"
- Down arrow to Node 3: "Heisenberg voltages and spectral sampling \n Circuit separation and full regular operator norm control"
- Down arrow to Node 4: "Geometric realization \n Embedded sheets; disjoint domains; finite cycle coordinates"
- Two downward branching arrows from Node 4 to parallel nodes:
  - Left branch to Node 5a: "Integral detector and torsion control \n A primitive torus class in a torsion-free group"
  - Right branch to Node 5b: "Supported Bott homotopies \n Even and odd families agree; initial class vanishes"
- Two downward converging arrows from Node 5a and Node 5b to bottom node:
  - Node 6: "A finite witness gives a finitely generated subgroup \n $h \otimes 1 \ne 0 \text{ in } K_0(BG_{\mathrm{inj}}) \otimes \mathbb{Q}, \quad \mu_{G_{\mathrm{inj}}}^r(h) = 0$"

<a id="pm-h-6"></a>
#### Reading the construction

There are two indices: height runs through one infinite graph tower, while accuracy indexes a sequence of increasingly precise constructions. The graph degree is constant across heights in any one construction and may increase with accuracy. Section <a href="#sec:completion" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:completion">9</a> records the complete order of parameter choices after the required objects have been defined.

Sections <a href="#sec:labeling" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:labeling">2</a>–<a href="#sec:analysis" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:analysis">5</a> construct and sample the labeled voltage tower. Sections <a href="#sec:geometry" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:geometry">6</a>–<a href="#sec:domains" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:domains">7</a> realize it in a group and prove the cohomological detector and torsion claims. Section <a href="#sec:assembly" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:assembly">8</a> establishes the reduced Bott cancellation and extracts a finite witness. The final section puts these conclusions together with compatible parameters and identifies the detected torus Dirac class and its assembly image.

Throughout, all group $C^*$-algebras and all $K$-groups are complex. Commutators mean $[a,b]=aba^{-1}b^{-1}$. The graph construction uses binary logarithms. Matrix dimensions remain fixed along each approximation sequence in which quotient $K$-theory is used; finite generation is extracted only after an exact finite witness has been obtained.

<a id="sec:labeling"></a>
### A finite alphabet with deterministic recovery

We construct a labeled graph in layers, with the aim that every sufficiently long immersed word has only one occurrence. The alphabet must also remain small relative to the incidence degrees. The finite-alphabet separation objective is shared with Osajda’s construction (Osajda 2020, secs. 2.1–2.3, Theorem 2.7) <a id="pm-cite-24"></a>[[Osa20](#ref-Osa20)]; here the graph and its labels are built together, and uniqueness will follow from explicit recovery of vertex coordinates.

The local recovery step is elementary. Over a finite field, subtracting two evaluations $E_1=v+c_1u$ and $E_2=v+c_2u$ with $c_1\ne c_2$ determines $u$; either equation then determines $v$. We apply this observation block by block. Labels and previously recovered neighboring blocks will supply the evaluations. The rules below make this process compatible with partial terminal blocks and with comparisons between different heights.

A *slot* is a named candidate incidence at a vertex. Once the coefficient pools are fixed, the incidence, recovery, and vertex-deletion assertions in this section hold for every choice of permutation tables. Deletion fractions are counted using uniform vertex coordinates for each fixed table realization. The probability law on the tables is a separate choice, made in Section <a href="#sec:expansion" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:expansion">3</a>. All logarithms in this section have base two.

The padded-stream construction and recovery scheme adapt (OpenAI 2026, sec. 4 and 6) <a id="pm-cite-25"></a>[[OpenAIProjection](#ref-OpenAIProjection)]. The exponents, cutoff requirements, and gated corrections needed for the present interfaces are established below; the projection theorem is not an existence input.

<a id="pm-h-8"></a>
#### States, families, and extra coordinates

<a id="eq:stream-lengths"></a>
<a id="eq:source-order"></a>
<a id="eq:state-sizes"></a>
Fix a positive integer $b$. Let $s$ be a sufficiently large multiple of $20000$ and $5b$, and put <a id="eq:label-parameters"></a>$$\label{eq:label-parameters}
 r=\frac35s,\qquad d=2^{.40005s},\qquad
 D=2^{.60005s},\qquad q=2^{.50005s}.$$ The numbers $d,D$ will be the two incidence degrees in a fixed slice, and $q=\sqrt{dD}$ their geometric mean. The exponent choices allow both distinct coefficients for recovery and an alphabet whose square root is small compared with $q$; the precise alphabet estimate is <a href="#eq:sharp-alphabet" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:sharp-alphabet">[eq:sharp-alphabet]</a> below. These parameters stay fixed as the height $i$ varies. They may change when a later construction asks for a smaller approximation error. Choose an integer $n_0\ge 10^6$, to be increased later. For $i\ge0$ write $bi=2s a_i+t_i$, where $0\le t_i<2s$, and define $$\begin{aligned}
 \ell_A(i)&=r+n_0s+a_is+\max\{t_i-s,0\},\notag\\
 \ell_B(i)&=n_0s+a_is+\min\{t_i,s\},\label{eq:stream-lengths}\\
 \mathscr A_i&=\mathbb F_2^{\ell_A(i)}\times\mathbb F_2^{r+\ell_B(i)},\qquad
 \mathscr B_i=\mathbb F_2^{\ell_B(i)}\times\mathbb F_2^{s-r+\ell_A(i)}.\notag
\end{aligned}$$ The two coordinates are denoted $(u,v)$. Regard each as an infinite bit stream by adjoining zero coordinates. Both coordinates on a side use that side’s block convention: an A stream has an initial block $A_0$ of length $r$ and blocks $A_1,A_2,\ldots$ of length $s$; a B stream has blocks $B_1,B_2,\ldots$, all of length $s$. Index their interleaving by $$\label{eq:source-order}
 A_0\ (0),\ B_1\ (1),\ A_1\ (2),\ B_2\ (3),\ A_2\ (4),\ldots.$$ For the pair of spaces at height $i$, the active coordinates of $u_A$ and $u_B$ together form an initial segment of this interleaving. Its length and the logarithmic state size are $$\label{eq:state-sizes}
 \begin{aligned}
 L_i&=r+2n_0s+bi,&
 H_i&=L_i+s-r=(2n_0+1)s+bi,\\
 |\mathscr B_i|&=2^{H_i},&
 |\mathscr A_i|&=2^{H_i+2r-s}.
 \end{aligned}$$ Set $m_i=H_i/\log q$. We require, by increasing $s$, <a id="eq:label-slope"></a>$$\label{eq:label-slope}
 a:=\frac b{\log q}\le10^{-6}.$$

Let $\mathcal T$ be any fixed finite set of incidence types. A type $\tau$ specifies an offset $\delta_\tau\in\{-1,0,1\}$ and occurs between $\mathscr A_i$ and $\mathscr B_{i+\delta_\tau}$ whenever both indices are nonnegative. Put $T=|\mathcal T|$ and <a id="eq:family-modulus"></a>$$\label{eq:family-modulus}
 M=2\cdot10^6s.$$ The family of this pair is $f=(\tau,i\bmod M)$. Thus there are at most $TM$ families. Its two endpoint residues, its smaller height $h=\min\{i,i+\delta_\tau\}$ modulo $M$, and the phase of its cutoff are determined by $f$.

For an adjacent pair, truncate the larger endpoint to the coordinate lengths at height $h$. Exactly $b$ coordinates are discarded: they belong to its $u$ coordinate if that side’s $u$ stream grows from $h$ to $h+1$, and otherwise to its $v$ coordinate. They form one interval of $b$ bits, since $s,r$ and every active length are multiples of $b$. Denote their value by $e\in\mathbb F_2^b$. For an equal-height pair let $e$ be the empty string. In a fixed $e$ slice, both truncated endpoint spaces are exactly the spaces at height $h$ in <a href="#eq:stream-lengths" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:stream-lengths">[eq:stream-lengths]</a>.

<a id="eq:positive-label"></a>
At an A endpoint a slot has serial number $j\in\{1,\ldots,d\}$; at a B endpoint it has, in addition, a bucket $c\in\mathbb F_2^{2r-s}$. The type is always part of a slot name. At the smaller endpoint of an adjacent pair, $e$ is also part of its slot name. At the larger endpoint it is read from the state. Every positive incidence is oriented from its $\mathscr A$ endpoint to its $\mathscr B$ endpoint. A positive edge label will be $$\label{eq:positive-label}
 \lambda=(f,j,e,h_0),\qquad h_0=c\Vert h_0',\quad
 |h_0|=r,\quad |h_0'|=s-r.$$ Negative labels are formal inverses. The notation $h_0$ here is a label head, not a height.

<a id="eq:mark-spacing"></a>
For each family, mark its common $u$ cutoff $L_h$ at every actual occurrence. Let $\kappa$ be the source-block index of the last active common $u$ block and $t\in\{b,2b,\ldots,s\}$ its active prefix length. Also mark the interval of extra coordinates at each adjacent occurrence. For consecutive occurrences of one family both side lengths increase by $Mb/2$, and the indices $\kappa$ increase by $$\label{eq:mark-spacing}
 K=Mb/s=2\cdot10^6b.$$ The marked prefix length and its side therefore remain the same within a family. The raw $u$ data of its actual endpoint pair extend at most one source block beyond its common cutoff. An extra $v$ interval lies at most two source blocks beyond that cutoff, when its coordinate blocks are indexed by <a href="#eq:source-order" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:source-order">[eq:source-order]</a>.

<a id="pm-h-9"></a>
#### Finite coefficient pools

<a id="eq:name-count"></a>
An A coefficient name is $(f,j,e)$, and a B coefficient name is $(f,j,c,e)$. We include every legal value, including values of $e$ read from the larger state. In particular an A name does not contain the bucket, which has not yet been read when its label head is computed. Let $\mathcal N_A,\mathcal N_B$ be these two finite sets. Their sizes satisfy $$\label{eq:name-count}
 |\mathcal N_A|\le TM2^b d,\qquad
 |\mathcal N_B|\le TM2^bD.$$ Choose an injection of $\mathcal N_A$ into $\mathbb F_{2^r}$ and an injection of $\mathcal N_A\sqcup\mathcal N_B$ into $\mathbb F_{2^s}$. Identify these fields linearly with their bit-coordinate spaces. The first injection acts on block $A_0$; the second acts on every full $s$-block. For a name $\nu$, write $K_\nu$ for the block-diagonal map that multiplies each block by its assigned field element, expressed in bit coordinates. The same coefficients are used on every repeated block. The finite fields used here introduce no group elements: for each $h$, the roots of $z^{2^h}-z$ in a splitting field over $\mathbb F_2$ form a field of $2^h$ elements, since the polynomial has derivative $-1$ and its roots are closed under the field operations.

Distinct coefficients make the two-evaluation recovery step possible. Expansion requires a second property: after restricting to an active prefix, no output functional should give the same input functional on too many slots. The next lemma supplies this multiplicity bound as well as distinctness; Section <a href="#sec:expansion" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:expansion">3</a> uses it to estimate the mean incidence operator.

<a id="stmt-lem-coefficient-pools"></a>
<div id="lem:coefficient-pools" class="lemma">

**Lemma 2** (Coefficient choice). *For sufficiently large $s$, the injections can be chosen so that, within every fixed family and extra slice and on either side, the following holds. If $n$ is its number of slots, $\zeta$ is any nonzero output linear functional on an ordinary $s$-block, and $b\le t\le s$, then each restricted input functional $$(\zeta K_\nu)|_{\mathbb F_2^t}$$ occurs on at most $2^{1-b}n$ slots. All distinct names have distinct coefficients in every field in which they occur. The analogous bound holds on the initial A field for every prefix length between $b$ and $r$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-coefficient-pools"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-coefficient-pools</summary>

*Proof.* The injections exist because the polynomial factor $TM$ and fixed factor $2^b$ in <a href="#eq:name-count" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:name-count">[eq:name-count]</a> are dominated by the positive exponent gaps $r-.40005s$ and $s-.60005s$. Choose both injections uniformly. For fixed nonzero $\zeta$, multiplication identifies the field coefficients bijectively with all output-to-input functionals. On any specified set of $n$ names these are therefore sampled without replacement. Restriction to $t$ input bits has fibers of proportion $2^{-t}\le2^{-b}$.

Here is the required tail estimate. For $n$ zero–one samples without replacement, with sum $S$ and mean success proportion $p$, one has $$\mathbb E\exp(u(S-np))\le\exp(nu^2/8).$$ Indeed the exponential moment before centering is an elementary symmetric mean in the population entries $1,e^u$. Replacing two unequal nonnegative entries by their average increases every elementary symmetric sum: the only changing term is their product multiplied by a nonnegative symmetric sum in the other entries. Iteration compares the moment with sampling with replacement. For a centered Bernoulli variable, the second derivative of the logarithmic moment generating function is a tilted variance, at most $1/4$; integrating twice proves the displayed bound. Exponential Markov inequality gives $$\Pr\{S-np\ge 2^{-b}n\}\le
 \exp(-2^{1-2b}n).$$ There are at most $s2^{2s}$ choices of an output functional, prefix length, and restricted value. Multiplying by the polynomial number of families and the fixed number of extra slices and sides still gives $2^{O(s)}$ tests. Each has $n\ge d=2^{.40005s}$. Their total failure probability tends to zero. Fix a choice for which none fails. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-10"></a>
#### Universal corrections and permutation rules

The edge equations will couple each affine evaluation to a permuted input block at the opposite endpoint. To compare occurrences at different heights, we use one system of equations on arbitrary padded streams. The corrections remove the extra coordinates of an adjacent-height pair, while the gates and identity blocks preserve the padded tail. Their dependence on earlier raw inputs is what will let recovery proceed one block at a time.

<a id="eq:lag-gate"></a>
We now specify these rules on arbitrary padded raw $u_A,u_B$ streams, not only on actual endpoint states. A pair of full raw blocks means $(B_j,A_j)$ for $j\ge1$. For a source-block index $k>20$, put $J(k)=\lfloor(k-5)/2\rfloor$ and let $$\label{eq:lag-gate}
 g_k=1\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
 (u_{B,J(k)-3},u_{A,J(k)-3},\ldots,
   u_{B,J(k)},u_{A,J(k)})\ne0.$$ For $k\le20$ put $g_k=1$. Thus every noninitial gate reads exactly eight full raw blocks and reads no source-block index greater than $k-5$. In particular a correction to an evaluation block cannot consult the source block that will be recovered from that evaluation.

Fix a positive label <a href="#eq:positive-label" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:positive-label">[eq:positive-label]</a>. At every marked extra interval of its family, XOR $e$ into that raw coordinate interval if $g_k=1$, where $k$ is the coordinate-block index containing the interval. There are no corrections for an equal-height family. Denote the resulting streams by <a id="eq:corrected-streams"></a>$$\label{eq:corrected-streams}
 \bar u_C=u_C+C^u_{C,\lambda},\qquad
 \bar v_C=v_C+C^v_{C,\lambda},\qquad C\in\{A,B\}.$$ The sums are XOR sums; each finite prefix uses finitely many disjoint marked intervals. Every correction offset in a block is determined by the label and raw $u$ blocks strictly before both blocks coupled by the edge equation below. This extra lag is deliberate.

For every family, label, source-block index $k$, and tuple of all raw $u$ blocks strictly earlier than $k$ in <a href="#eq:source-order" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:source-order">[eq:source-order]</a>, provide a permutation table. At an unmarked index it is a permutation of the full block; at a marked cutoff with active length $t$ it is a permutation of $\mathbb F_2^t$. These are distinct variables for distinct complete keys. There are finitely many variables at each source-block index.

The transformation at a source block is defined as follows.

1.  At each marked cutoff use its prefix table on the first $t$ bits and the identity on the suffix, if $g_k=1$; if $g_k=0$, use the identity on the entire block.

2.  At the twenty source blocks immediately following every marked cutoff, use the identity unconditionally.

3.  At every other source block use its full-block table if $g_k=1$, and otherwise the identity.

<a id="eq:universal-edge-equations"></a>
The marked and following intervals do not overlap by <a href="#eq:mark-spacing" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:mark-spacing">[eq:mark-spacing]</a>. Tables act on corrected $\bar u$ blocks, while their keys and gates read raw preceding $u$ blocks. Write $P_{A,\lambda}(\bar u_A)$ and $P_{B,\lambda}(\bar u_B)$ for the two transformed streams. For a fixed slot write $Q_C=\bar v_C+K_\nu\bar u_C$ for its corrected evaluation; the slot and label subscripts will usually be suppressed. Its difference from the raw evaluation $v_C+K_\nu u_C$ is the known correction offset $C^v_{C,\lambda}+K_\nu C^u_{C,\lambda}$. The universal edge equations are $$\label{eq:universal-edge-equations}
 \boxed{\quad
 \bar v_A+K_{(f,j,e)}\bar u_A
       =h_0\Vert P_{B,\lambda}(\bar u_B),\qquad
 \bar v_B+K_{(f,j,c,e)}\bar u_B
       =h_0'\Vert P_{A,\lambda}(\bar u_A).
 \quad}$$ They are equations of infinite padded streams.

For the auxiliary incidence at an actual pair, modify these rules at that occurrence only: force its extra correction on, if there is one, and force its current final-prefix permutation on. All lower rules retain their gates. Denote this auxiliary prescription by a superscript $0$. We shall delete states for which either forced choice was necessary. The sparse unconditional identity zones replace any need for a post-cutoff gate that could inadvertently read the raw extra bits.

<a id="pm-h-11"></a>
#### Auxiliary incidences and uniform coordinates

The auxiliary prescription gives a finite incidence at every height pair. We first verify that its equations preserve the required supports, then solve them in either direction. The same inversion will identify the uniform coordinates used to count gate failures.

<a id="stmt-lem-incidence-inversion"></a>
<div id="lem:incidence-inversion" class="lemma">

**Lemma 3** (Inversion and uniform coordinates). *For every choice of tables the auxiliary prescription has exactly one edge at every specified endpoint and slot. In a fixed extra slice it is biregular of degrees $d,D$. A uniform endpoint with a fixed slot, after fixing its extra value when necessary, is parametrized bijectively by the free label head and all free raw $u_A,u_B$ coordinates at the common cutoff. Consequently those raw coordinates are independent uniform bits.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-incidence-inversion"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-incidence-inversion</summary>

*Proof.* *Preservation of the common support.* At the actual larger endpoint the forced correction cancels its extra bits. Lower correction intervals lie in active coordinates. Higher correction gates vanish: their eight preceding raw blocks are wholly beyond the actual endpoint support, because consecutive marks are separated by $K>20$ source blocks. Thus corrected streams have exactly the common permitted lengths.

The transformed corrected $u$ streams preserve these lengths. At the last common block the suffix is the identity. The next twenty blocks are identity, and their corrected inputs are zero. Raw $u$ support extends by at most one block beyond the common cutoff. At every later ordinary index the gate window <a href="#eq:lag-gate" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:lag-gate">[eq:lag-gate]</a> is entirely beyond that support, so its gate is closed. The same holds at all higher marked rules. This proves preservation of the padded tail, including an extra interval in the larger endpoint’s $u$ coordinate.

<a id="eq:terminal-shapes"></a>
For clarity, the two possible common terminal shapes are $$\label{eq:terminal-shapes}
\begin{array}{c|cccc}
 &\ell_A&\ell_B&|v_A|&|v_B|\\ \hline
 \text{last B prefix }t&r+ns&ns+t&r+ns+t&(n+1)s\\
 \text{last A prefix }t&r+ns+t&(n+1)s&r+(n+1)s&(n+1)s+t .
\end{array}$$ In both rows $1\le t\le s$ and the output of each block-diagonal $K$ fits in the displayed $v$ length. If an extra interval is in $v$, it lies beyond this output: its same-side direct block is zero there. Thus computing $v$ from the equations cannot leak a $K u$ term into the prescribed extra interval.

*Inversion from either endpoint.* Given an A endpoint and slot, its extra value is known, either from the state or from the slot. Its first $r$ evaluation bits determine $h_0$; there are no marked corrections in the initial blocks. Hence $c$ and the B coefficient name are known. Proceed through the B blocks. To compute its next corrected evaluation from the A endpoint, all correction gates use already known raw blocks by their five-index lag. The B permutation key uses only earlier B blocks and the preceding A block. Invert that known block permutation, then undo the known correction to recover the next raw B $u$ block. The other equation now determines its raw $v$ block. Continue in order. The support argument just given shows that all recovered streams have the required lengths.

Conversely a B slot gives $c$. Its first $s-r$ evaluation bits give $h_0'$, and hence $h_0$. Inverting successive A block permutations recovers raw $u_A$ and then $v_A$. These two procedures solve the same triangular equations and are inverse. The lag is important here: a correction in $Q_{B,j}$ does not read the unknown $u_{A,j-1}$, and one in $Q_{A,j}$ does not read the unknown $u_{B,j}$.

*Uniform coordinates.* First freely specify the raw $u$ blocks on both sides, omitting only the larger endpoint’s fixed extra interval if that interval is in $u$. Specify $h_0$ for an A slot, or $h_0'$ for a B slot whose bucket is fixed. Every gate, correction, and permutation value is now determined. Equations <a href="#eq:universal-edge-equations" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:universal-edge-equations">[eq:universal-edge-equations]</a> determine both $\bar v$ streams, and undoing the corrections determines both raw $v$ streams. A forced extra interval in $v$ is exactly $e$. The number of free coordinates is $$\ell_A(h)+\ell_B(h)+r=\log|\mathscr A_h|
 \quad\hbox{or}\quad
 \ell_A(h)+\ell_B(h)+s-r=\log|\mathscr B_h|,$$ respectively. The preceding inversion proves bijectivity, not merely equality of dimensions. Uniform measure therefore becomes product uniform measure in these coordinates. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-12"></a>
#### Deleting gate failures

Let $X^0$ be the union of the auxiliary incidences over all types and height pairs. At a slot, evaluate the natural gates on its auxiliary edge, even where the auxiliary prescription forced them on.
